## Artificer

_Expansion class — Eberron: Rising from the Last War (2019), reprinted for general use in Tasha's Cauldron of Everything (2020). Artificer has never been reprinted in 2024-rules language, so this entry keeps its original phrasing; parenthetical notes below flag the places that read differently from this compendium's other, 2024-style classes._

**Core Artificer Traits**

| Col 1 | Col 2 |
| --- | --- |
| Primary Ability | Intelligence |
| Hit Point Die | D8 per Artificer level |
| Saving Throw Proficiencies | Constitution and Intelligence |
| Skill Proficiencies | Choose 2: Arcana, History, Investigation, Medicine, Nature, Perception, or Sleight of Hand |
| Weapon Proficiencies | Simple weapons |
| Armor Training | Light and Medium armor and Shields |
| Tool Proficiencies | Thieves' Tools, Tinker's Tools, and one type of Artisan's Tools of your choice |
| Starting Equipment | Choose A or B: (A) a Simple weapon, Thieves' Tools, Tinker's Tools, a set of Artisan's Tools of your choice, Studded Leather armor, Dungeoneer's Pack, and 25 GP; or (B) 75 GP |

_Multiclassing note (2024 phrasing note: the 2024 PHB moved most multiclassing prerequisites onto Background/level-1 choices rather than ability-score gates on other classes; this legacy class keeps its original prerequisite)._ Taking your first Artificer level, or taking a level in another class after already having levels in Artificer, requires Intelligence 13 or higher. Multiclassing into Artificer grants proficiency with Light armor, Medium armor, and Shields, plus proficiency with Thieves' Tools, Tinker's Tools, and one type of Artisan's Tools of your choice.

### Artificer Class Features

As an Artificer, you gain the following class features when you reach the specified Artificer levels.

**Artificer Features**

| Level | Proficiency Bonus | Class Features | Cantrips Known | Infusions Known | Infused Items | Prepared Spells | 1st | 2nd | 3rd | 4th | 5th |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | +2 | Magical Tinkering, Spellcasting | 2 | — | — | 1 | 2 | — | — | — | — |
| 2 | +2 | Infuse Item | 2 | 4 | 2 | 1 | 2 | — | — | — | — |
| 3 | +2 | Artificer Specialist, The Right Tool for the Job | 2 | 4 | 2 | 1 | 3 | — | — | — | — |
| 4 | +2 | Ability Score Improvement | 2 | 4 | 2 | 2 | 3 | — | — | — | — |
| 5 | +3 | Artificer Specialist feature | 2 | 4 | 2 | 2 | 4 | 2 | — | — | — |
| 6 | +3 | Tool Expertise | 2 | 6 | 3 | 3 | 4 | 2 | — | — | — |
| 7 | +3 | Flash of Genius | 2 | 6 | 3 | 3 | 4 | 3 | — | — | — |
| 8 | +3 | Ability Score Improvement | 2 | 6 | 3 | 4 | 4 | 3 | — | — | — |
| 9 | +4 | Artificer Specialist feature | 2 | 6 | 3 | 4 | 4 | 3 | 2 | — | — |
| 10 | +4 | Magic Item Adept | 3 | 8 | 4 | 5 | 4 | 3 | 2 | — | — |
| 11 | +4 | Spell-Storing Item | 3 | 8 | 4 | 5 | 4 | 3 | 3 | — | — |
| 12 | +4 | Ability Score Improvement | 3 | 8 | 4 | 6 | 4 | 3 | 3 | — | — |
| 13 | +5 | — | 3 | 8 | 4 | 6 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 1 | — |

| 15 | +5 | Artificer Specialist feature | 4 | 10 | 5 | 7 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 2 | — |
| 16 | +5 | Ability Score Improvement | 4 | 10 | 5 | 8 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 2 | — |
| 17 | +6 | — | 4 | 10 | 5 | 8 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 1 |

_(2024 phrasing note: this class predates the 2024 PHB's Epic Boon convention, so it keeps its original design of a fifth Ability Score Improvement at level 19 instead of an Epic Boon feat at that level.)_

#### Level 1: Magical Tinkering

As an action, you touch a Tiny nonmagical object and imbue it with one of the following magical properties of your choice: it sheds Bright Light in a 5-foot radius and Dim Light for an additional 5 feet; it emits a recorded message for up to 6 seconds whenever it's touched or a proximity condition you set is met; it produces a continuous, harmless sensory effect, such as a sound, an odor, or a visual pattern; or its exterior takes on a static, cosmetic visual effect of your choice, such as a color, a pattern, or an image. The chosen property lasts indefinitely, ending if you use this feature again or if you dismiss it (no action required). You can bestow this property on a number of objects at once equal to your Intelligence modifier (minimum of one).

#### Level 1: Spellcasting

You have learned to channel magic through your inventions. See "Spells" for the rules on spellcasting; the information below details how you use those rules with Artificer spells.

**Tools Required.** You produce your Artificer spell effects through your tools rather than a spellbook or an Arcane Focus. You must have Thieves' Tools or a set of Artisan's Tools with which you have proficiency in hand when you cast an Artificer spell with a casting time of an action, a Bonus Action, or a Reaction.

**Cantrips.** You know two cantrips of your choice from the Artificer spell list. You learn additional Artificer cantrips of your choice at higher levels, as shown in the Cantrips Known column of the Artificer Features table.

**Spell Slots.** The Artificer Features table shows how many spell slots you have to cast your level 1+ spells. You regain all expended slots when you finish a Long Rest.

**Prepared Spells of Level 1+.** Unlike other spellcasters, you don't fix your list of known spells; instead, you have access to the entire Artificer spell list from level 1, and you choose which of those spells to prepare each day. The number of spells you can prepare equals your Intelligence modifier plus half your Artificer level (round down, minimum of one); the Prepared Spells column of the Artificer Features table shows this number assuming an Intelligence modifier of +0, add your own modifier to it. The chosen spells must be of a level for which you have spell slots. Whenever you finish a Long Rest, you can change your list of prepared spells.

If another Artificer feature gives you spells that you always have prepared, those spells don't count against the number of spells you can prepare with this feature, but they otherwise count as Artificer spells for you.

**Spellcasting Ability.** Intelligence is your spellcasting ability for your Artificer spells.

**Ritual Casting.** You can cast an Artificer spell you have prepared as a Ritual if that spell has the Ritual tag.

#### Level 2: Infuse Item

You've learned how to imbue mundane items with magical infusions.

**Infusions Known.** You know a number of infusion formulas equal to the Infusions Known column of the Artificer Features table, chosen from the infusions list below. Whenever you gain an Artificer level, you can replace one formula you know with a different one.

**Infusing Items.** Whenever you finish a Long Rest, you can touch a nonmagical object and imbue it with one of your infusions, turning it into a magic item; you must spend some of the rest's time working on the item to do so. An infusion has no effect on a creature or an object that isn't allowed by its description, and a legendary or artifact item can never be infused. You determine the properties of an infused item when you infuse it, and once infused, that item's magical properties remain fixed until you replace the infusion.

You can have a number of items infused at a time equal to the Infused Items column of the Artificer Features table. If you try to exceed your maximum, the oldest infusion ends and the item reverts to being nonmagical.

**Infusions List.** Your infusion formulas are chosen from the following list; several become available only at higher Artificer levels.

**Enhanced Defense** (level 2). A suit of armor or a Shield grants a +1 bonus to Armor Class; this bonus increases to +2 at Artificer level 10.

**Enhanced Weapon** (level 2). A Weapon grants a +1 bonus to attack and damage rolls; this bonus increases to +2 at level 10.

**Enhanced Arcane Focus** (level 2). An Arcane Focus grants a +1 bonus to spell attack rolls, and the wielder can ignore Half Cover when making a spell attack; the bonus increases to +2 at level 10.

**Repeating Shot** (level 2). A nonmagical weapon that has the Ammunition property grants a +1 bonus to ranged attack and damage rolls made with it, ignores its Loading property if it has one, and manifests its own magical ammunition, which vanishes immediately after the attack; the bonus increases to +2 at level 10.

**Returning Weapon** (level 2). A Melee weapon that has the Thrown property gains a +1 bonus to attack and damage rolls, and it flies back to your hand immediately after you use it to make a ranged attack.

**Armor of Magical Strength** (level 2). A suit of armor stores 6 charges. The wearer can expend 1 charge to add your Intelligence modifier to a Strength check or a Strength saving throw, or to avoid being knocked Prone. The armor regains 1d6 expended charges daily at dawn.

**Mind Sharpener** (level 2). A suit of armor gains 4 charges. When the wearer fails a Constitution saving throw to maintain Concentration, they can expend 1 charge to succeed instead. The armor regains 1d4 expended charges daily at dawn.

**Homunculus Servant** (level 2). You create a Tiny construct companion (see its stat block below).

**Replicate Magic Item** (level 2, expanding at levels 6, 10, and 14). You can infuse a formula that replicates a specific common magic item (uncommon starting at level 6, rare starting at level 10, very rare starting at level 14) from a fixed list keyed to your Artificer level.

**Boots of the Winding Path** (level 6). As a Bonus Action, the wearer can teleport up to 15 feet to an unoccupied space they've occupied at some point during the current turn.

**Radiant Weapon** (level 6). A Weapon grants a +1 bonus to attack and damage rolls. As a Bonus Action, the wielder can cause it to shed Bright Light in a 30-foot radius and Dim Light for an additional 30 feet. The weapon stores 4 charges; when hit by an attacker within 30 feet, the wielder can take a Reaction and expend 1 charge to force that attacker to make a Constitution saving throw or have the Blinded condition until the start of the wielder's next turn. The weapon regains all expended charges daily at dawn.

**Repulsion Shield** (level 6). A Shield grants a +1 bonus to Armor Class. It stores 4 charges; when a creature within 5 feet hits the wearer with a melee attack, the wearer can expend 1 charge (no action required) to push that creature up to 15 feet away. The shield regains 1d4 expended charges daily at dawn.

**Resistant Armor** (level 6). A suit of armor grants Resistance to one damage type you choose from Acid, Cold, Fire, Force, Lightning, Necrotic, Poison, Psychic, Radiant, or Thunder.

**Spell-Refueling Ring** (level 6). As an action, the wearer can expend the ring's charge to regain one expended spell slot of level 3 or lower. The ring regains its charge daily at dawn.

**Helm of Awareness** (level 10). The wearer has Advantage on Initiative rolls and can't have the Surprised condition while conscious.

**Arcane Propulsion Armor** (level 14). A suit of armor grants the wearer a +5-foot bonus to Speed. It includes magic gauntlets that deal 1d8 Force damage and can be thrown, returning to the wearer's hand after the attack. The armor can't be removed against the wearer's will, and it can replace a missing arm or leg.

#### Level 3: Artificer Specialist

You gain an Artificer specialization of your choice: Alchemist, Armorer, Artillerist, or Battle Smith, all detailed after this class's description. Your specialization grants you features at levels 3, 5, 9, and 15.

#### Level 3: The Right Tool for the Job

With Tinker's Tools in hand, you can magically create one set of Artisan's Tools of a kind you don't currently own, in an unoccupied space within 5 feet of yourself. Doing so requires 1 hour of work, which can be done during a Short Rest. You can have only one set of tools created this way at a time; creating a new set causes the previous set to disappear.

#### Level 4: Ability Score Improvement

You gain the Ability Score Improvement feat (see "Feats") or another feat of your choice for which you qualify. You gain this feature again at Artificer levels 8, 12, 16, and 19.

#### Level 6: Tool Expertise

Your Proficiency Bonus is doubled for any ability check you make that uses a tool with which you're proficient.

#### Level 7: Flash of Genius

When you or a creature within 30 feet of yourself makes an ability check or a saving throw, you can take a Reaction to add your Intelligence modifier to the roll. You can use this feature a number of times equal to your Intelligence modifier (minimum of once), and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

#### Level 10: Magic Item Adept

You can attune to up to 4 magic items at once. In addition, you can craft Common and Uncommon magic items in a quarter of the normal crafting time, and for half the normal gold cost.

#### Level 11: Spell-Storing Item

During a Long Rest, you can store a level 1 or 2 spell that has a casting time of an action into a Simple or Martial weapon, or into an item you can use as a Spellcasting Focus. A creature holding the item can take an action to produce the spell's effect from it, using your spell save DC and spell attack bonus. The item retains enough magic for two uses; the process must be repeated during a Long Rest to restore its charges or to store a different spell.

#### Level 14: Magic Item Savant

Your attunement limit rises to 5 magic items at once. In addition, you ignore all class, race, spell, and level requirements for attuning to or using a magic item.

#### Level 18: Magic Item Master

Your attunement limit rises to 6 magic items at once.

#### Level 20: Soul of Artifice

You gain a +1 bonus to all saving throws for each magic item you are currently attuned to. In addition, if you're reduced to 0 Hit Points but not killed outright, you can take a Reaction to end one of your own infusions; if you do, you drop to 1 Hit Point instead of 0.

**Homunculus Servant.** Your Homunculus Servant infusion creates a Tiny construct with Armor Class 13 (natural armor); Hit Points equal to 1 plus your Intelligence modifier plus your Artificer level; a Speed of 20 feet and a Fly Speed of 30 feet; and a _Force Strike_ attack (a ranged spell attack using your spell attack modifier, range 30 feet, dealing 1d4 plus your Proficiency Bonus in Force damage on a hit). It can take a Reaction, called _Channel Magic_, to deliver a touch-range spell you cast, provided it's within 120 feet of you when you cast the spell.

### Artificer Subclass: Alchemist

_Discover Elixirs and Remedies_

Alchemists are Artificers who specialize in the creation of magical elixirs, drawing on chemistry and alchemy to heal allies and harm foes alike.

#### Level 3: Tools of the Trade

You gain proficiency with Alchemist's Supplies. If you already have this proficiency, you instead gain proficiency with one other type of Artisan's Tools of your choice.

#### Level 3: Alchemist Spells

You always have certain spells prepared, and they don't count against the number of spells you can prepare. When you reach a character level specified in the Alchemist Spells table, you thereafter always have the listed spells prepared.

**Alchemist Spells**

| Character Level | Spells |
| --- | --- |
| 3 | [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Healing Word\|Healing Word]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Ray of Sickness\|Ray of Sickness]] |
| 5 | [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Flaming Sphere\|Flaming Sphere]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Acid Arrow\|Melf's Acid Arrow]] |
| 9 | [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Gaseous Form\|Gaseous Form]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Mass Healing Word\|Mass Healing Word]] |
| 13 | [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Blight\|Blight]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Death Ward\|Death Ward]] |
| 17 | [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Cloudkill\|Cloudkill]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Raise Dead\|Raise Dead]] |

#### Level 3: Experimental Elixir

Whenever you finish a Long Rest, you can magically produce one experimental elixir for free. You can also create additional elixirs by expending a spell slot for each one, up to a number of extra elixirs equal to your Intelligence modifier (minimum of zero) per day. Each elixir lasts until it's used or until you finish your next Long Rest.

As an action, a creature can drink an elixir to gain a random effect from the Experimental Elixir table below, determined by rolling a d6.

**Experimental Elixir**

| d6 | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | Healing. The drinker regains 2d4 plus your Intelligence modifier in Hit Points. |
| 2 | Boldness. The drinker can roll a d4 and add the number rolled to every attack roll and saving throw they make for the next minute. |
| 3 | Swiftness. The drinker's Speed increases by 10 feet for 1 hour. |
| 4 | Resilience. The drinker gains a +1 bonus to Armor Class for 10 minutes. |
| 5 | Flight. The drinker gains a Fly Speed of 10 feet for 10 minutes. |
| 6 | Transformation. The drinker's body is transformed as though by the [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Alter Self\|Alter Self]] spell for 10 minutes; the drinker chooses the effect. |

#### Level 5: Alchemical Savant

When you cast an Artificer spell using Alchemist's Supplies as your Spellcasting Focus, you can add your Intelligence modifier (minimum of +1) to one damage or healing roll of that spell, provided the spell deals Acid, Fire, Necrotic, or Poison damage, or restores Hit Points.

#### Level 9: Restorative Reagents

Any creature that drinks one of your Experimental Elixirs also gains Temporary Hit Points equal to 2d6 plus your Intelligence modifier. In addition, you can cast [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Lesser Restoration|Lesser Restoration]] without expending a spell slot, using Alchemist's Supplies as a Spellcasting Focus, a number of times equal to your Intelligence modifier (minimum of once), and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

#### Level 15: Chemical Mastery

You have Resistance to Acid and Poison damage, and Immunity to the Poisoned condition. You can cast [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Greater Restoration|Greater Restoration]] or [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Heal|Heal]] once each, without expending a spell slot or requiring Material components, using Alchemist's Supplies as a Spellcasting Focus, and you regain the ability to cast each spell this way when you finish a Long Rest.

### Artificer Subclass: Armorer

_Turn Your Own Armor into a Weapon_

Armorers are Artificers who transform their own armor into an extension of their magic, called Arcane Armor, built for either close-quarters defense or ranged infiltration.

#### Level 3: Tools of the Trade

You gain proficiency with Heavy armor and with Smith's Tools. If you already have proficiency with Smith's Tools, you instead gain proficiency with one other type of Artisan's Tools of your choice.

#### Level 3: Armorer Spells

You always have certain spells prepared, and they don't count against the number of spells you can prepare. When you reach a character level specified in the Armorer Spells table, you thereafter always have the listed spells prepared.

**Armorer Spells**

| Character Level | Spells |
| --- | --- |
| 3 | [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Magic Missile\|Magic Missile]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Thunderwave\|Thunderwave]] |
| 5 | [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Mirror Image\|Mirror Image]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Shatter\|Shatter]] |
| 9 | [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Hypnotic Pattern\|Hypnotic Pattern]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Lightning Bolt\|Lightning Bolt]] |
| 13 | [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Fire Shield\|Fire Shield]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Greater Invisibility\|Greater Invisibility]] |
| 17 | [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Passwall\|Passwall]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Wall of Force\|Wall of Force]] |

#### Level 3: Arcane Armor

As an action, with Smith's Tools in hand, you can turn armor you're wearing into Arcane Armor. It has no Strength requirement for you, it doesn't impose Disadvantage on Dexterity (Stealth) checks beyond what your chosen Armor Model allows, and it can serve as your Spellcasting Focus for Artificer spells. Your Arcane Armor attaches to you: it can't be removed against your will, you can expand or retract its helmet as a Bonus Action, and it can replace a missing arm or leg. Doffing or donning it takes an action. It remains your Arcane Armor until you attune a different suit of armor to replace it, or until you die.

When you gain this feature, and again whenever you finish a Short or Long Rest with Smith's Tools in hand, you can change your armor's Armor Model between the two options below.

**Guardian.**

_Thunder Gauntlets._ Your Arcane Armor includes a built-in Simple Melee weapon, usable even while your hands are full; you're always considered to have a free hand to wield it. It deals 1d8 Thunder damage, using your Intelligence modifier instead of Strength for its attack and damage rolls. A creature hit by it has Disadvantage on attack rolls against creatures other than you until the start of your next turn.

_Defensive Field._ As a Bonus Action, you can grant yourself Temporary Hit Points equal to your Artificer level; this doesn't stack with Temporary Hit Points you already have. You can use this benefit a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest. You lose access to this benefit if you take off your Arcane Armor.

**Infiltrator.**

_Lightning Launcher._ Your Arcane Armor includes a built-in Simple Ranged weapon mounted on an arm or on your chest, with a range of 90/300 feet, dealing 1d6 Lightning damage using your Intelligence modifier instead of Dexterity for its attack and damage rolls. Once per turn when you hit with it, you can deal an extra 1d6 Lightning damage.

_Powered Steps._ Your Speed increases by 5 feet.

_Dampening Field._ You have Advantage on Dexterity (Stealth) checks, and any Disadvantage your armor would normally impose on such checks is negated.

#### Level 5: Extra Attack

You can attack twice, instead of once, whenever you take the Attack action on your turn.

#### Level 9: Armor Modifications

Your Arcane Armor now counts as four separate items for the purpose of your Infuse Item feature: the armor itself, its boots, its helmet, and its special weapon. Each of these can hold its own infusion, and any infusions transfer automatically when you change your Armor Model. Your maximum number of infused items increases by 2, usable only on components of your Arcane Armor.

#### Level 15: Perfected Armor

Your Armor Model gains an additional benefit, depending on which you've chosen.

**Guardian.** As a Reaction when a Huge or smaller creature ends its turn within 30 feet of yourself, you can force it to make a Strength saving throw against your spell save DC. On a failed save, you pull it up to 25 feet straight toward you, into an unoccupied space; if it ends within 5 feet of you, you can make one Melee weapon attack against it as part of the same Reaction. You can use this benefit a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

**Infiltrator.** A creature damaged by your Lightning Launcher glimmers with light until the start of your next turn: it sheds Dim Light in a 5-foot radius, it has Disadvantage on attack rolls against you, and the next attack roll made against it has Advantage and, on a hit, deals an extra 1d6 Lightning damage.

### Artificer Subclass: Artillerist

_Build a Magical Cannon of Force and Flame_

Artillerists are Artificers who focus their creativity on ranged magical firepower, conjuring a small but devastating eldritch cannon to fight alongside them.

#### Level 3: Tools of the Trade

You gain proficiency with Woodcarver's Tools. If you already have this proficiency, you instead gain proficiency with one other type of Artisan's Tools of your choice.

#### Level 3: Artillerist Spells

You always have certain spells prepared, and they don't count against the number of spells you can prepare. When you reach a character level specified in the Artillerist Spells table, you thereafter always have the listed spells prepared.

**Artillerist Spells**

| Character Level | Spells |
| --- | --- |
| 3 | [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Shield\|Shield]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Thunderwave\|Thunderwave]] |
| 5 | [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Scorching Ray\|Scorching Ray]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Shatter\|Shatter]] |
| 9 | [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Fireball\|Fireball]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Wind Wall\|Wind Wall]] |
| 13 | [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Ice Storm\|Ice Storm]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Wall of Fire\|Wall of Fire]] |
| 17 | [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Cone of Cold\|Cone of Cold]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Wall of Force\|Wall of Force]] |

#### Level 3: Eldritch Cannon

As an action, with Woodcarver's Tools or Smith's Tools in hand, you can magically create a Small or Tiny eldritch cannon in an unoccupied space within 5 feet of yourself, choosing one of the three types below; you can't change its type once created. The cannon has Armor Class 18 and Hit Points equal to five times your Artificer level, and it's Immune to Poison and Psychic damage. It disappears after 1 hour, when reduced to 0 Hit Points, when you dismiss it (an action), or when you die. You can have only one eldritch cannon at a time; creating a new one destroys an old one.

As a Bonus Action, provided the cannon is within 60 feet of yourself, you can command it to take an action, such as activating one of its effects below. You can activate a cannon a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

**Flamethrower.** The cannon exhales fire in a 15-foot Cone. Each creature in that area must make a Dexterity saving throw against your spell save DC, taking 2d8 Fire damage on a failed save, or half as much damage on a successful one.

**Force Ballista.** The cannon makes a ranged spell attack, using your spell attack modifier, against one target within 120 feet, dealing 2d8 Force damage on a hit and pushing the target up to 5 feet away from the cannon.

**Protector.** The cannon emits a burst of positive energy that grants itself and each creature of your choice within 10 feet of it Temporary Hit Points equal to 1d8 plus your Intelligence modifier.

#### Level 5: Arcane Firearm

During a Short or Long Rest, you can imbue a Wand, a Staff, or a Rod you're carrying with a fraction of your magic, making it your Spellcasting Focus for Artificer spells and letting you roll a d8 and add it to one damage roll of an Artificer spell you cast through it.

#### Level 9: Explosive Cannon

All damage rolls made by your Eldritch Cannon increase by 1d8. In addition, as an action while within 60 feet of your cannon, you can command it to detonate; it's destroyed, and each creature within 20 feet of it must make a Dexterity saving throw against your spell save DC, taking 3d8 Force damage on a failed save, or half as much damage on a successful one.

#### Level 15: Fortified Position

You and your allies have Half Cover while within 10 feet of your eldritch cannon. In addition, you can have two eldritch cannons active at once, and you can activate both with a single Bonus Action.

### Artificer Subclass: Battle Smith

_Fight Alongside a Steel Defender_

Battle Smiths are Artificers who craft a construct companion, the Steel Defender, to fight and protect at their side.

#### Level 3: Tools of the Trade

You gain proficiency with Smith's Tools. If you already have this proficiency, you instead gain proficiency with one other type of Artisan's Tools of your choice.

#### Level 3: Battle Smith Spells

You always have certain spells prepared, and they don't count against the number of spells you can prepare. When you reach a character level specified in the Battle Smith Spells table, you thereafter always have the listed spells prepared.

**Battle Smith Spells**

| Character Level | Spells |
| --- | --- |
| 3 | [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Heroism\|Heroism]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Shield\|Shield]] |
| 5 | [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Branding Smite\|Branding Smite]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Warding Bond\|Warding Bond]] |
| 9 | [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Aura of Vitality\|Aura of Vitality]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Conjure Barrage\|Conjure Barrage]] |
| 13 | [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Aura of Purity\|Aura of Purity]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Fire Shield\|Fire Shield]] |
| 17 | [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Banishing Smite\|Banishing Smite]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Mass Cure Wounds\|Mass Cure Wounds]] |

#### Level 3: Battle Ready

You gain proficiency with Martial weapons. In addition, when you attack with a magic weapon, you can use your Intelligence modifier, instead of Strength or Dexterity, for its attack and damage rolls.

#### Level 3: Steel Defender

You gain a Medium construct companion, the Steel Defender, that is Friendly to you and your allies and acts on your turn. You determine its outward appearance, such as whether it has two or four legs; this choice is cosmetic.

**Steel Defender.** Armor Class 15 (natural armor); Hit Points equal to 2 plus your Intelligence modifier plus five times your Artificer level; Speed 40 feet. Ability scores: Strength 14, Dexterity 12, Constitution 14, Intelligence 4, Wisdom 10, Charisma 6. It has Dexterity and Constitution saving throw proficiency (ability modifier plus your Proficiency Bonus), Athletics proficiency (Strength modifier plus your Proficiency Bonus), and Perception proficiency at double your Proficiency Bonus. It has Immunity to Poison damage and to the Charmed, Exhausted, and Poisoned conditions, Darkvision with a range of 60 feet, and it can't be Surprised.

_Force-Empowered Rend._ As an action, the defender makes a Melee attack, reach 5 feet, using your spell attack modifier, dealing 1d8 plus your Proficiency Bonus in Force damage on a hit.

_Repair._ Three times per day, as an action, the defender can restore 2d8 plus your Proficiency Bonus in Hit Points to itself or to another construct or object within 5 feet of itself.

_Deflect Attack._ As a Reaction when a creature the defender can see hits it with an attack roll, the defender imposes Disadvantage on that attack roll.

#### Level 5: Extra Attack

You can attack twice, instead of once, whenever you take the Attack action on your turn.

#### Level 9: Arcane Jolt

When you hit a target with a magic weapon attack, or when your Steel Defender hits a target with its Force-Empowered Rend, you can channel magical energy into the strike: either deal an extra 2d6 Force damage to the target, or restore 2d6 Hit Points to a creature within 30 feet of the target. You can use this feature a number of times equal to your Intelligence modifier (minimum of once), and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest; you can use it only once per turn.

#### Level 15: Improved Defender

Your Arcane Jolt's extra damage or healing increases to 4d6. Your Steel Defender's Armor Class increases by 2. In addition, whenever your Steel Defender uses its Deflect Attack feature, the attacking creature takes 1d4 plus your Intelligence modifier in Force damage.

## Barbarian

**Core Barbarian Traits**

| Col 1 | Col 2 |
| --- | --- |
| Primary Ability | Strength |
| Hit Point Die | D12 per Barbarian level |
| Saving Throw Proficiencies | Strength and Constitution |
| Skill Proficiencies | Choose 2: Animal Handling, Athletics, Intimidation, Nature, Perception, or Survival |
| Weapon Proficiencies | Simple and Martial weapons |
| Armor Training | Light and Medium armor and Shields |
| Starting Equipment | Choose A or B: (A) Greataxe, 4 Handaxes, Explorer's Pack, and 15 GP; or (B) 75 GP |

### Becoming a Barbarian …

#### As a Level 1 Character

• Gain all the traits in the Core Barbarian Traits table.

• Gain the Barbarian's level 1 features, which are listed in the Barbarian Features table.

#### As a Multiclass Character

• Gain the following traits from the Core Barbarian Traits table: Hit Point Die, proficiency with Martial weapons, and training with Shields.
• Gain the Barbarian's level 1 features, which are listed in the Barbarian Features table.

### Barbarian Class Features

As a Barbarian, you gain the following class features when you reach the specified Barbarian levels. These features are listed in the Barbarian Features table.

**Barbarian Features**

| Level | Proficiency Bonus | Class Features | Rages | Rage Damage | Weapon Mastery |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | +2 | Rage, Unarmored Defense, Weapon Mastery | 2 | +2 | 2 |
| 2 | +2 | Danger Sense, Reckless Attack | 2 | +2 | 2 |
| 3 | +2 | Barbarian Subclass, Primal Knowledge | 3 | +2 | 2 |
| 4 | +2 | Ability Score Improvement | 3 | +2 | 3 |
| 5 | +3 | Extra Attack, Fast Movement | 3 | +2 | 3 |
| 6 | +3 | Subclass feature | 4 | +2 | 3 |
| 7 | +3 | Feral Instinct, Instinctive Pounce | 4 | +2 | 3 |
| 8 | +3 | Ability Score Improvement | 4 | +2 | 3 |
| 9 | +4 | Brutal Strike | 4 | +3 | 3 |
| 10 | +4 | Subclass feature | 4 | +3 | 4 |
| 11 | +4 | Relentless Rage | 4 | +3 | 4 |
| 12 | +4 | Ability Score Improvement | 5 | +3 | 4 |
| 13 | +5 | Improved Brutal Strike | 5 | +3 | 4 |
| 14 | +5 | Subclass feature | 5 | +3 | 4 |
| 15 | +5 | Persistent Rage | 5 | +3 | 4 |
| 16 | +5 | Ability Score Improvement | 5 | +4 | 4 |
| 17 | +6 | Improved Brutal Strike | 6 | +4 | 4 |
| 18 | +6 | Indomitable Might | 6 | +4 | 4 |
| 19 | +6 | Epic Boon | 6 | +4 | 4 |
| 20 | +6 | Primal Champion | 6 | +4 | 4 |

#### Level 1: Rage

You can imbue yourself with a primal power called Rage, a force that grants you extraordinary might and resilience. You can enter it as a Bonus Action if you aren't wearing Heavy armor.

You can enter your Rage the number of times shown for your Barbarian level in the Rages column of the Barbarian Features table. You regain one expended use when you finish a Short Rest, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

While active, your Rage follows the rules below.

_Damage Resistance._ You have Resistance to Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing damage.

_Rage Damage._ When you make an attack using Strength—with either a weapon or an Unarmed Strike—and deal damage to the target, you gain a bonus to the damage that increases as you gain levels as a Barbarian, as shown in the Rage Damage column of the Barbarian Features table.

_Strength Advantage._ You have Advantage on Strength checks and Strength saving throws.

_No Concentration or Spells._ You can't maintain Concentration, and you can't cast spells.

_Duration._ The Rage lasts until the end of your next turn, and it ends early if you don Heavy armor or have the Incapacitated condition. If your Rage is still active on your next turn, you can extend the Rage for another round by doing one of the following:

- Make an attack roll against an enemy.
- Force an enemy to make a saving throw.
- Take a Bonus Action to extend your Rage.

Each time the Rage is extended, it lasts until the end of your next turn. You can maintain a Rage for up to 10 minutes.

#### Level 1: Unarmored Defense

While you aren't wearing any armor, your base Armor Class equals 10 plus your Dexterity and Constitution modifiers. You can use a Shield and still gain this benefit.

#### Level 1: Weapon Mastery

Your training with weapons allows you to use the mastery properties of two kinds of Simple or Martial Melee weapons of your choice, such as Greataxes and Handaxes. Whenever you finish a Long Rest, you can practice weapon drills and change one of those weapon choices.

When you reach certain Barbarian levels, you gain the ability to use the mastery properties of more kinds of weapons, as shown in the Weapon Mastery column of the Barbarian Features table.

#### Level 2: Danger Sense

You gain an uncanny sense of when things aren't as they should be, giving you an edge when you dodge perils. You have Advantage on Dexterity saving throws unless you have the Incapacitated condition.

#### Level 2: Reckless Attack

You can throw aside all concern for defense to attack with increased ferocity. When you make your first attack roll on your turn, you can decide to attack recklessly. Doing so gives you Advantage on attack rolls using Strength until the start of your next turn, but attack rolls against you have Advantage during that time.

#### Level 3: Barbarian Subclass

You gain a Barbarian subclass of your choice. The Path of the Berserker subclass is detailed after this class's description. A subclass is a specialization that grants you features at certain Barbarian levels. For the rest of your career, you gain each of your subclass's features that are of your Barbarian level or lower.

#### Level 3: Primal Knowledge

You gain proficiency in another skill of your choice from the skill list available to Barbarians at level 1.

In addition, while your Rage is active, you can channel primal power when you attempt certain tasks; whenever you make an ability check using one of the following skills, you can make it as a Strength check even if it normally uses a different ability: Acrobatics, Intimidation, Perception, Stealth, or Survival. When you use this ability, your Strength represents primal power coursing through you, honing your agility, bearing, and senses.

#### Level 4: Ability Score Improvement

You gain the Ability Score Improvement feat (see "Feats") or another feat of your choice for which you qualify. You gain this feature again at Barbarian levels 8, 12, and 16.

#### Level 5: Extra Attack

You can attack twice instead of once whenever you take the Attack action on your turn.

#### Level 5: Fast Movement

Your speed increases by 10 feet while you aren't wearing Heavy armor.

#### Level 7: Feral Instinct

Your instincts are so honed that you have Advantage on Initiative rolls.

#### Level 7: Instinctive Pounce

As part of the Bonus Action you take to enter your Rage, you can move up to half your Speed.

#### Level 9: Brutal Strike

If you use Reckless Attack, you can forgo any Advantage on one Strength-based attack roll of your choice on your turn. The chosen attack roll mustn't have Disadvantage. If the chosen attack roll hits, the target takes an extra 1d10 damage of the same type dealt by the weapon or Unarmed Strike, and you can cause one Brutal Strike effect of your choice. You have the following effect options.

_Forceful Blow._ The target is pushed 15 feet straight away from you. You can then move up to half your Speed straight toward the target without provoking Opportunity Attacks.
_Hamstring Blow._ The target's Speed is reduced by 15 feet until the start of your next turn. A target can be affected by only one Hamstring Blow at a time—the most recent one.

#### Level 11: Relentless Rage

Your Rage can keep you fighting despite grievous wounds. If you drop to 0 Hit Points while your Rage is active and don't die outright, you can make a DC 10 Constitution saving throw. If you succeed, your Hit Points instead change to a number equal to twice your Barbarian level.

Each time you use this feature after the first, the DC increases by 5. When you finish a Short or Long Rest, the DC resets to 10.

#### Level 13: Improved Brutal Strike

You have honed new ways to attack furiously. The following effects are now among your Brutal Strike options.

_Staggering Blow._ The target has Disadvantage on the next saving throw it makes, and it can't make Opportunity Attacks until the start of your next turn.

_Sundering Blow._ Before the start of your next turn, the next attack roll made by another creature against the target gains a +5 bonus to the roll. An attack roll can gain only one Sundering Blow bonus.

#### Level 15: Persistent Rage

When you roll Initiative, you can regain all expended uses of Rage. After you regain uses of Rage in this way, you can't do so again until you finish a Long Rest.

In addition, your Rage is so fierce that it now lasts for 10 minutes without you needing to do anything to extend it from round to round. Your Rage ends early if you have the Unconscious condition (not just the Incapacitated condition) or don Heavy armor.

#### Level 17: Improved Brutal Strike

The extra damage of your Brutal Strike increases to 2d10. In addition, you can use two different Brutal Strike effects whenever you use your Brutal Strike feature.

#### Level 18: Indomitable Might

If your total for a Strength check or Strength saving throw is less than your Strength score, you can use that score in place of the total.

#### Level 19: Epic Boon

You gain an Epic Boon feat (see "Feats") or another feat of your choice for which you qualify. Boon of Irresistible Offense is recommended.

#### Level 20: Primal Champion

You embody primal power. Your Strength and Constitution scores increase by 4, to a maximum of 25.

### Barbarian Subclass: Path of the Berserker

_Channel Rage into Violent Fury_

Barbarians who walk the Path of the Berserker direct their Rage primarily toward violence. Their path is one of untrammeled fury, and they thrill in the chaos of battle as they allow their Rage to seize and empower them.

#### Level 3: Frenzy

If you use Reckless Attack while your Rage is active, you deal extra damage to the first target you hit on your turn with a Strength-based attack. To determine the extra damage, roll a number of d6s equal to your Rage Damage bonus, and add them together. The damage has the same type as the weapon or Unarmed Strike used for the attack.

#### Level 6: Mindless Rage

You have Immunity to the Charmed and Frightened conditions while your Rage is active. If you're Charmed or Frightened when you enter your Rage, the condition ends on you.

#### Level 10: Retaliation

When you take damage from a creature that is within 5 feet of you, you can take a Reaction to make one melee attack against that creature, using a weapon or an Unarmed Strike.

#### Level 14: Intimidating Presence

As a Bonus Action, you can strike terror into others with your menacing presence and primal power. When you do so, each creature of your choice in a 30-foot Emanation originating from you must make a Wisdom saving throw (DC 8 plus your Strength modifier and Proficiency Bonus). On a failed save, a creature has the Frightened condition for 1 minute. At the end of each of the Frightened creature's turns, the creature repeats the save, ending the effect on itself on a success.

Once you use this feature, you can't use it again until you finish a Long Rest unless you expend a use of your Rage (no action required) to restore your use of it.

### Barbarian Subclass: Path of the Wild Heart

_Feel the Spirits of the Wild_

Barbarians who walk the Path of the Wild Heart feel a deep spiritual kinship with animals, and their Rage channels that bond into supernatural gifts drawn from beasts such as the bear, eagle, and wolf.

#### Level 3: Animal Speaker

You can cast _Beast Sense_ and [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Speak with Animals|Speak with Animals]] with this trait, but only as Rituals. Wisdom is your spellcasting ability for these spells.

#### Level 3: Rage of the Wilds

Whenever you activate your Rage, you can choose an animal aspect from the options below, granting you supernatural benefits until the Rage ends.

**Bear.** While raging, you have Resistance to every damage type except Force, Necrotic, Psychic, and Radiant.

**Eagle.** As part of the Bonus Action you use to activate your Rage, you can take the Disengage and Dash actions. While your Rage is active, you can take a Bonus Action to take the Disengage and Dash actions again.

**Wolf.** While your Rage is active, your allies have Advantage on attack rolls against any enemy of yours that is within 5 feet of you.

#### Level 6: Aspect of the Wilds

You gain a magical benefit based on an animal aspect. Choose one of the following options; you can change your choice whenever you finish a Long Rest.

**Owl.** You have Darkvision with a range of 60 feet. If you already have Darkvision, its range increases by 60 feet.

**Panther.** You have a Climb Speed equal to your Speed.

**Salmon.** You have a Swim Speed equal to your Speed.

#### Level 10: Nature Speaker

You can cast [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Commune with Nature|Commune with Nature]] with this trait, but only as a Ritual. Wisdom is your spellcasting ability for this spell.

#### Level 14: Power of the Wilds

Whenever you activate your Rage, you can also choose one of the following animal aspects, in addition to any Rage of the Wilds aspect, granting you supernatural benefits until the Rage ends.

**Falcon.** While you aren't wearing armor, you have a Fly Speed equal to your Speed.

**Lion.** Enemies within 5 feet of you have Disadvantage on attack rolls against creatures other than you or another creature with this aspect active.

**Ram.** When you hit a Large or smaller creature with a melee attack, you can give that creature the Prone condition.

### Barbarian Subclass: Path of the World Tree

_Draw Strength from Yggdrasil_

Barbarians who walk the Path of the World Tree draw on the cosmic power of Yggdrasil, the tree that connects the planes of existence, channeling it into battlefield vitality, protective roots, and short-range teleportation.

#### Level 3: Vitality of the Tree

You gain the following benefits.

_Vitality Surge._ Immediately after you activate your Rage, you gain Temporary Hit Points equal to your Barbarian level.

_Life-Giving Force._ At the start of each of your turns while your Rage is active, choose yourself or a creature within 10 feet of you. Roll a number of d6s equal to your Rage Damage bonus, and the chosen creature gains that many Temporary Hit Points.

#### Level 6: Branches of the Tree

When a creature you can see starts its turn within 30 feet of you while your Rage is active, you can take a Reaction to command spectral roots to lash out. That creature must succeed on a Strength saving throw (DC 8 plus your Strength modifier and Proficiency Bonus) or be teleported to an unoccupied space within 5 feet of you (or the nearest unoccupied space) and have its Speed reduced to 0 until the end of the current turn.

#### Level 10: Battering Roots

Melee weapons that have the Heavy or Versatile property have their reach increased by 10 feet when you wield them. In addition, when you hit a creature with such a weapon, you can apply the Push or Topple mastery property to the attack, even if you're already applying a different mastery property with that weapon.

#### Level 14: Travel along the Tree

When you activate your Rage, or as a Bonus Action while your Rage is active, you can teleport up to 60 feet to an unoccupied space you can see. Once per Rage, you can instead teleport up to 150 feet in this way, bringing with you up to six willing creatures within 10 feet of you, placing them in unoccupied spaces of your choice within 10 feet of your destination space.

### Barbarian Subclass: Path of the Zealot

_Fight as a Weapon of the Gods_

Barbarians who walk the Path of the Zealot are chosen instruments of their deities, channeling their Rage into divine violence and shrugging off wounds that would fell lesser warriors.

#### Level 3: Divine Fury

While your Rage is active, the first creature you hit on each of your turns with a weapon or an Unarmed Strike takes extra damage equal to 1d6 plus half your Barbarian level (round down). You choose whether this extra damage is Necrotic or Radiant damage each time you deal it.

#### Level 3: Warrior of the Gods

You have a pool of 4d12 that you can spend, as a Bonus Action, to heal yourself, drawing on your god's power. Roll the dice you spend from the pool, and you regain that many Hit Points. This pool increases to 5d12 when you reach Barbarian level 6, 6d12 at level 12, and 7d12 at level 17. You regain all expended dice from this pool when you finish a Long Rest.

#### Level 6: Fanatical Focus

Once per Rage, if you fail a saving throw, you can reroll it, and you must use the new roll.

#### Level 10: Zealous Presence

As a Bonus Action, you invoke divine fervor in up to ten creatures you can see within 60 feet of yourself, granting Advantage on attack rolls and saving throws to those creatures until the start of your next turn. Once you use this feature, you can't use it again until you finish a Long Rest unless you expend a use of your Rage (no action required) to restore your use of it.

#### Level 14: Rage of the Gods

When you activate your Rage, you can transform into divine, spectral form for 1 minute or until you drop to 0 Hit Points. While transformed, you have a Fly Speed equal to your Speed (with the ability to hover), and you have Resistance to Necrotic, Psychic, and Radiant damage.

While transformed, you can take a Reaction to expend a use of your Rage when a creature within 30 feet of you drops to 0 Hit Points; instead, that creature drops to a number of Hit Points equal to your Barbarian level.

Once you transform in this way, you can't do so again until you finish a Long Rest.

### Barbarian Subclass: Path of the Beast

_Expansion subclass — Tasha's Cauldron of Everything (2020). Not one of the four core 2024 PHB Barbarian subclasses (Berserker/Wild Heart/World Tree/Zealot)—kept here as bonus content._

_Unleash a Bestial Transformation_

Barbarians who walk the Path of the Beast carry a primal, bestial power within them, one that manifests as natural weapons and a feral fury when their Rage takes hold.

#### Level 3: Form of the Beast

When you enter your Rage, you can manifest a natural weapon as part of that Bonus Action; it disappears when your Rage ends. Choose one of the following forms each time you Rage.

**Bite.** A bestial maw or mandibles, functioning as a Simple Melee weapon that deals 1d8 Piercing damage. Once per turn, when you hit a creature with your Bite while you're Bloodied, you regain Hit Points equal to your Proficiency Bonus.

**Claws.** Your claws deal 1d6 Slashing damage on a hit. Once per turn when you take the Attack action and attack with your claws, you can make one additional claw attack as part of that same action.

**Tail.** Your tail deals 1d8 Piercing damage and has the Reach property, extending your reach by 10 feet. As a Reaction when a creature within 10 feet of yourself hits you with an attack roll, you can swipe with your tail, rolling a d8 and adding the result as a bonus to your Armor Class against that attack, potentially turning the hit into a miss.

#### Level 6: Bestial Soul

Your natural weapons from Form of the Beast count as magical for the purpose of overcoming Resistance and Immunity to nonmagical attacks and damage. In addition, whenever you finish a Short or Long Rest, you can choose one of the following benefits, which lasts until you finish your next rest.

_Aquatic Adaptation._ You gain a Swim Speed equal to your Speed, and you can breathe underwater.

_Climbing Adaptation._ You gain a Climb Speed equal to your Speed.

_Leaping Adaptation._ When you make a running Long or High Jump, you can add the result of a Strength (Athletics) check to the distance, rather than just your Strength score. You can use this benefit once per turn.

#### Level 10: Infectious Fury

When you hit a creature with your natural weapon from Form of the Beast while your Rage is active, you can force that creature to make a Wisdom saving throw (DC 8 plus your Constitution modifier and Proficiency Bonus). On a failed save, choose one of the following effects: the target must use its Reaction, if it has one available, to make a melee attack against a creature of your choice other than itself; or the target takes 2d12 Psychic damage. On a successful save, if the target is within 30 feet of you, you can instead deal 1d12 Psychic damage to it. You can use this feature a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

#### Level 14: Call the Hunt

When you activate your Rage, you can call out to up to a number of willing creatures within 30 feet of yourself equal to your Constitution modifier (minimum of one), designating them as part of your hunt. Each of those creatures gains 5 Temporary Hit Points. Until your Rage ends, each of those creatures can, once per turn when it hits a creature with an attack, roll a d6 and add the number rolled to the damage dealt. You can use this feature a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

## Bard

**Core Bard Traits**

| Col 1 | Col 2 |
| --- | --- |
| Primary Ability | Charisma |
| Hit Point Die | D8 per Bard level |
| Saving Throw Proficiencies | Dexterity and Charisma |
| Skill Proficiencies | Choose any 3 skills (see Playing the Game) |
| Weapon Proficiencies | Simple weapons |
| Tool Proficiencies | Choose 3 Musical Instruments (see Equipment) |
| Armor Training | Light armor |
| Starting Equipment | Choose A or B: (A) Leather Armor, 2 Daggers, Musical Instrument of your choice, Entertainer's Pack, and 19 GP; or (B) 90 GP |

### Becoming a Bard …

#### As a Level 1 Character

• Gain all the traits in the Core Bard Traits table.

• Gain the Bard's level 1 features, which are listed in the Bard Features table.

#### As a Multiclass Character

• Gain the following traits from the Core Bard Traits table: Hit Point Die, proficiency in one skill of your choice, proficiency with one Musical Instrument of your choice, and training with Light armor.
• Gain the Bard's level 1 features, which are listed in the Bard Features table. See the multiclassing rules in "Character Creation" to determine your available spell slots.

### Bard Class Features

As a Bard, you gain the following class features when you reach the specified Bard levels. These features are listed in the Bard Features table.

**Bard Features**

| Level | Proficiency Bonus | Class Features | Bardic Die | Cantrips | Prepared Spells | Lvl 1 Slots | Lvl 2 Slots | Lvl 3 Slots | Lvl 4 Slots | Lvl 5 Slots | Lvl 6 Slots | Lvl 7 Slots | Lvl 8 Slots | Lvl 9 Slots |   |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | +2 | Bardic Inspiration, Spellcasting | D6 | 2 | 4 | 2 | — | — | — | — | — | — | — | — |   |
| 2 | +2 | Expertise, Jack of All Trades | D6 | 2 | 5 | 3 | — | — | — | — | — | — | — | — |   |
| 3 | +2 | Bard Subclass | D6 | 2 | 6 | 4 | 2 | — | — | — | — | — | — | — |   |
| 4 | +2 | Ability Score Improvement | D6 | 3 | 7 | 4 | 3 | — | — | — | — | — | — | — |   |
| 5 | +3 | Font of Inspiration | D8 | 3 | 9 | 4 | 3 | 2 | — | — | — | — | — | — |   |
| 6 | +3 | Subclass feature | D8 | 3 | 10 | 4 | 3 | 3 | — | — | — | — | — | — |   |
| 7 | +3 | Countercharm | D8 | 3 | 11 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 1 | — | — | — | — | — |   |
| 8 | +3 | Ability Score Improvement | D8 | 3 | 12 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 2 | — | — | — | — | — |   |
| 9 | +4 | Expertise | D8 | 3 | 14 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 1 | — | — | — | — |   |
| 10 | +4 | Magical Secrets | D10 | 4 | 15 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 2 | — | — | — | — |   |
| 11 | +4 | — | D10 | 4 | 16 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 2 | 1 | — | — | — |   |
| 12 | +4 | Ability Score Improvement | D10 | 4 | 16 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 2 | 1 | — | — | — |   |
| 13 | +5 | — | D10 | 4 | 17 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 2 | 1 | 1 | — | — |   |
| 14 | +5 | Subclass feature | D10 | 4 | 17 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 2 | 1 | 1 | — | — |   |
| 15 | +5 | — | D12 | 4 | 18 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 2 | 1 | 1 | 1 | — |   |
| 16 | +5 | Ability Score Improvement | D12 | 4 | 18 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 2 | 1 | 1 | 1 | — |   |
| 17 | +6 | — | D12 | 4 | 19 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 2 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 |   |
| 18 | +6 | Superior Inspiration | D12 | 4 | 20 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 |   |
| 19 | +6 | Epic Boon | D12 | 4 | 21 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 2 | 1 | 1 | 1 |   |
| 20 | +6 | Words of Creation | D12 | 4 | 22 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 2 | 2 | 1 | 1 |   |

#### Level 1: Bardic Inspiration

You can supernaturally inspire others through words, music, or dance. This inspiration is represented by your Bardic Inspiration die, which is a d6.

_Using Bardic Inspiration._ As a Bonus Action, you can inspire another creature within 60 feet of yourself who can see or hear you. That creature gains one of your Bardic Inspiration dice. A creature can have only one Bardic Inspiration die at a time.

Once within the next hour when the creature fails a D20 Test, the creature can roll the Bardic Inspiration die and add the number rolled to the d20, potentially turning the failure into a success. A Bardic Inspiration die is expended when it's rolled.

_Number of Uses._ You can confer a Bardic Inspiration die a number of times equal to your Charisma modifier (minimum of once), and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

_At Higher Levels._ Your Bardic Inspiration die changes when you reach certain Bard levels, as shown in the Bardic Die column of the Bard Features table. The die becomes a d8 at level 5, a d10 at level 10, and a d12 at level 15.

#### Level 1: Spellcasting

You have learned to cast spells through your bardic arts. See "Spells" for the rules on spellcasting. The information below details how you use those rules with Bard spells, which appear in the Bard spell list later in the class's description.

_Cantrips._ You know two cantrips of your choice from the Bard spell list. [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Dancing Lights|Dancing Lights]] and [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Vicious Mockery|Vicious Mockery]] are recommended.

Whenever you gain a Bard level, you can replace one of your cantrips with another cantrip of your choice from the Bard spell list.

When you reach Bard levels 4 and 10, you learn another cantrip of your choice from the Bard spell list, as shown in the Cantrips column of the Bard Features table.

_Spell Slots._ The Bard Features table shows how many spell slots you have to cast your level 1+ spells. You regain all expended slots when you finish a Long Rest.

_Prepared Spells of Level 1+._ You prepare the list of level 1+ spells that are available for you to cast with this feature. To start, choose four level 1 spells from the Bard spell list. [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Charm Person|Charm Person]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Color Spray|Color Spray]], [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Dissonant Whispers|Dissonant Whispers]], and [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Healing Word|Healing Word]] are recommended.

The number of spells on your list increases as you gain Bard levels, as shown in the Prepared Spells column of the Bard Features table. Whenever that number increases, choose additional spells from the Bard spell list until the number of spells on your list matches the number on the table. The chosen spells must be of a level for which you have spell slots. For example, if you're a level 3 Bard, your list of prepared spells can include six spells of levels 1 and 2 in any combination.

If another Bard feature gives you spells that you always have prepared, those spells don't count against the number of spells you can prepare with this feature, but those spells otherwise count as Bard spells for you.

_Changing Your Prepared Spells._ Whenever you gain a Bard level, you can replace one spell on your list with another Bard spell for which you have spell slots.

_Spellcasting Ability._ Charisma is your spellcasting ability for your Bard spells.

_Spellcasting Focus._ You can use a Musical Instrument as a Spellcasting Focus for your Bard spells.

#### Level 2: Expertise

You gain Expertise (see "Rules Glossary") in two of your skill proficiencies of your choice. Performance and Persuasion are recommended if you have proficiency in them.

At Bard level 9, you gain Expertise in two more of your skill proficiencies of your choice.

#### Level 2: Jack of All Trades

You can add half your Proficiency Bonus (round down) to any ability check you make that uses a skill proficiency you lack and that doesn't otherwise use your Proficiency Bonus.

For example, if you make a Strength (Athletics) check and lack Athletics proficiency, you can add half your Proficiency Bonus to the check.

#### Level 3: Bard Subclass

You gain a Bard subclass of your choice. The College of Lore subclass is detailed after this class's description. A subclass is a specialization that grants you features at certain Bard levels. For the rest of your career, you gain each of your subclass's features that are of your Bard level or lower.

#### Level 4: Ability Score Improvement

You gain the Ability Score Improvement feat (see "Feats") or another feat of your choice for which you qualify. You gain this feature again at Bard levels 8, 12, and 16.

#### Level 5: Font of Inspiration

You now regain all your expended uses of Bardic Inspiration when you finish a Short or Long Rest.

In addition, you can expend a spell slot (no action required) to regain one expended use of Bardic Inspiration.

#### Level 7: Countercharm

You can use musical notes or words of power to disrupt mind-influencing effects. If you or a creature within 30 feet of you fails a saving throw against an effect that applies the Charmed or Frightened condition, you can take a Reaction to cause the save to be rerolled, and the new roll has Advantage.

#### Level 10: Magical Secrets

You've learned secrets from various magical traditions. Whenever you reach a Bard level (including this level) and the Prepared Spells number in the Bard Features table increases, you can choose any of your new prepared spells from the Bard, Cleric, Druid, and Wizard spell lists, and the chosen spells count as Bard spells for you (see a class's section for its spell list). In addition, whenever you replace a spell prepared for this class, you can replace it with a spell from those lists.

#### Level 18: Superior Inspiration

When you roll Initiative, you regain expended uses of Bardic Inspiration until you have two if you have fewer than that.

#### Level 19: Epic Boon

You gain an Epic Boon feat (see "Feats") or another feat of your choice for which you qualify. Boon of Spell Recall is recommended.

#### Level 20: Words of Creation

You have mastered two of the Words of Creation: the words of life and death. You therefore always have the [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Power Word Heal|Power Word Heal]] and [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Power Word Kill|Power Word Kill]] spells prepared. When you cast either spell, you can target a second creature with it if that creature is within 10 feet of the first target.

### Bard Subclass: College of Dance

_Weave Magic into Movement_

Bards of the College of Dance treat their own bodies as instruments, weaving magic through footwork and turning graceful movement into a martial art. They train in courts and festivals alike, as comfortable leading a ballroom as they are leading a charge.

#### Level 3: Dazzling Footwork

While you aren't wearing armor and aren't using a Shield, you gain the following benefits.

_Dance Virtuoso._ You have Advantage on Charisma (Performance) checks that involve dancing.

_Unarmored Defense._ Your base Armor Class equals 10 plus your Dexterity modifier plus your Charisma modifier.

_Agile Strikes._ Immediately after you expend a use of your Bardic Inspiration die as part of an action, a Bonus Action, or a Reaction, you can make one Unarmed Strike as part of that same action, Bonus Action, or Reaction. You use Dexterity for the attack roll, and it deals Bludgeoning damage equal to a roll of your Bardic Inspiration die plus your Dexterity modifier.

#### Level 6: Inspiring Movement

When a creature you can see ends its turn within 5 feet of you, you can take a Reaction and expend a use of your Bardic Inspiration to move up to half your Speed without provoking Opportunity Attacks. When you do so, you can also grant the same benefit to one ally within 30 feet of you, who can use their Reaction to move up to half their Speed without provoking Opportunity Attacks.

#### Level 6: Tandem Footwork

When you roll Initiative, if you aren't Incapacitated, you can expend a use of your Bardic Inspiration; roll the die and add the number rolled to your Initiative roll and to the Initiative roll of any allies within 30 feet of you who can see or hear you.

#### Level 14: Leading Evasion

When you make a Dexterity saving throw to take half damage from an effect, you instead take no damage on a success and half damage on a failure. As long as you aren't Incapacitated, any creature within 5 feet of you making the same saving throw against the same effect gains this same benefit.

### Bard Subclass: College of Glamour

_Enchant with Feywild Beauty_

Bards of the College of Glamour learn their art from the fey courts of the Feywild, blending beauty and menace into performances that can charm a crowd or command a battlefield.

#### Level 3: Beguiling Magic

You always have the [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Charm Person|Charm Person]] and [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Mirror Image|Mirror Image]] spells prepared.

Immediately after you cast an Enchantment or Illusion spell using a spell slot, you can cause one creature you can see within 60 feet of yourself to make a Wisdom saving throw against your spell save DC. On a failed save, the target has the Charmed or Frightened condition (your choice) for 1 minute. The target repeats the save at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on itself on a success.

#### Level 3: Mantle of Inspiration

As a Bonus Action, you expend a use of your Bardic Inspiration and roll the die. You choose a number of creatures you can see within 60 feet of yourself, up to a number equal to your Charisma modifier (minimum of one). Each of those creatures gains Temporary Hit Points equal to twice the number rolled and can immediately use its Reaction to move up to its Speed without provoking Opportunity Attacks.

#### Level 6: Mantle of Majesty

You always have the [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Command|Command]] spell prepared.

As a Bonus Action, you cast [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Command|Command]] without expending a spell slot and take on an otherworldly, fey appearance for 1 minute or until your Concentration ends. For the duration, you can cast [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Command|Command]] (at will, no spell slot required) as a Bonus Action, and any creature Charmed by you automatically fails its saving throw against your castings of [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Command|Command]].

Once you use this feature, you can't use it again until you finish a Long Rest, unless you expend a spell slot of level 3 or higher (no action required) to restore your use of it.

#### Level 14: Unbreakable Majesty

As a Bonus Action, you assume a magically majestic presence for 1 minute or until you have the Incapacitated condition. For the duration, the first time on each of a creature's turns that it hits you with an attack roll, that creature must succeed on a Charisma saving throw against your spell save DC or the attack instead misses you.

Once you use this feature, you can't use it again until you finish a Short or Long Rest.

### Bard Subclass: College of Lore

_Plumb the Depths of Magical Knowledge_

Bards of the College of Lore collect spells and secrets from diverse sources, such as scholarly tomes, mystical rites, and peasant tales. The college's members gather in libraries and universities to share their lore with one another. They also meet at festivals or affairs of state, where they can expose corruption, unravel lies, and poke fun at self-important figures of authority.

#### Level 3: Bonus Proficiencies

You gain proficiency with three skills of your choice.

#### Level 3: Cutting Words

You learn to use your wit to supernaturally distract, confuse, and otherwise sap the confidence and competence of others. When a creature that you can see within 60 feet of yourself makes a damage roll or succeeds on an ability check or attack roll, you can take a Reaction to expend one use of your Bardic Inspiration; roll your Bardic Inspiration die, and subtract the number rolled from the creature's roll, reducing the damage or potentially turning the success into a failure.

#### Level 6: Magical Discoveries

You learn two spells of your choice. These spells can come from the Cleric, Druid, or Wizard spell list or any combination thereof (see a class's section for its spell list). A spell you choose must be a cantrip or a spell for which you have spell slots, as shown in the Bard Features table.

You always have the chosen spells prepared, and whenever you gain a Bard level, you can replace one of the spells with another spell that meets these requirements.

#### Level 14: Peerless Skill

When you make an ability check or attack roll and fail, you can expend one use of Bardic Inspiration; roll the Bardic Inspiration die, and add the number rolled to the d20, potentially turning a failure into a success. On a failure, the Bardic Inspiration isn't expended.

### Bard Subclass: College of Valor

_Sing of Battles Fought and Won_

Bards of the College of Valor are battlefield historians who fight alongside the heroes they'll later sing about, blending martial training with inspiring magic to bolster their allies in the thick of combat.

#### Level 3: Combat Inspiration

A creature holding a Bardic Inspiration die you granted it can roll that die and add the number rolled to its Armor Class against one attack that would otherwise hit it (using a Reaction), or the creature can add the number rolled to a damage roll of an attack it just hit with.

#### Level 3: Martial Training

You gain proficiency with Martial weapons, Medium armor, and Shields. You can also use a Simple or Martial weapon as a Spellcasting Focus for your Bard spells.

#### Level 6: Extra Attack

You can attack twice, instead of once, whenever you take the Attack action on your turn. Immediately after you take the Attack action on your turn, you can cast a Bard cantrip that has a casting time of an action as part of that same action, replacing one of your attacks.

#### Level 14: Battle Magic

Immediately after you cast a Bard spell with a casting time of an action, you can make one weapon attack as a Bonus Action.

### Bard Subclass: College of Whispers

_Expansion subclass — Xanathar's Guide to Everything (2017). Not one of the four core 2024 PHB Bard subclasses (Dance/Glamour/Lore/Valor)—kept here as bonus content._

_Wield Fear and Secrets as Weapons_

Bards of the College of Whispers trade in dread and secrets, using their performances to unsettle and manipulate rather than inspire.

#### Level 3: Psychic Blades

When you hit a creature with a weapon attack, you can expend one use of your Bardic Inspiration (spent on yourself rather than granted to an ally) to deal an extra 2d6 Psychic damage to that target, provided you can see it. You can do this only once per turn. This extra damage increases as you gain Bard levels: 3d6 at level 5, 5d6 at level 10, and 8d6 at level 15.

#### Level 3: Words of Terror

If you speak privately with a Humanoid for at least 1 minute, you can attempt to seed dread in its mind. At the end of the conversation, the target must succeed on a Wisdom saving throw against your spell save DC or have the Frightened condition toward you (or another creature you designate) for 1 hour. This effect ends early if the target is attacked or takes damage, or if it witnesses you or its allies attacking or damaging someone. If the target succeeds on the save, it has no idea you attempted this at all.

#### Level 6: Mantle of Whispers

Whenever a Humanoid dies within 30 feet of yourself, you can take a Reaction to magically capture its shadow as it expires. You can later take an action to adopt that shadow as a magical disguise, taking on the dead creature's appearance for 1 hour or until you dismiss it early (a Bonus Action). While disguised, you gain a general sense of the creature's mundane background and relationships, enough to convincingly pass as a casual acquaintance. If a creature interacts with you and suspects something is amiss, it can contest your Charisma (Deception) check with its own Wisdom (Insight) check; you gain a +5 bonus to your Deception check for this purpose. Once you capture a shadow, you can't capture another until you finish a Short or Long Rest.

#### Level 14: Shadow Lore

As an action, you can magically whisper a message that only one creature within 30 feet of yourself can hear, provided it can hear you at all (it automatically succeeds on the saving throw below if it can't hear you or doesn't share a language with you). The target must succeed on a Wisdom saving throw against your spell save DC or have the Charmed condition toward you for 8 hours. While Charmed this way, the target is convinced you know an embarrassing or ruinous secret about it (even though you don't) and acts on your suggestions and commands out of fear you'll expose it, including doing you favors or giving you gifts it would give to a close friend—though it won't act against its own self-preservation on your behalf unless it was already inclined to. Once you use this feature, you can't use it again until you finish a Long Rest.

## Cleric

**Core Cleric Traits**

| Col 1 | Col 2 |
| --- | --- |
| Primary Ability | Wisdom |
| Hit Point Die | D8 per Cleric level |
| Saving Throw Proficiencies | Wisdom and Charisma |
| Skill Proficiencies | Choose 2: History, Insight, Medicine, Persuasion, or Religion |
| Weapon Proficiencies | Simple weapons |
| Armor Training | Light and Medium armor and Shields |
| Starting Equipment | Choose A or B: (A) Chain Shirt, Shield, Mace, Holy Symbol, Priest's Pack, and 7 GP; or (B) 110 GP |

### Becoming a Cleric …

#### As a Level 1 Character

- Gain all the traits in the Core Cleric Traits table.
- Gain the Cleric's level 1 features, which are listed in the Cleric Features table.

#### As a Multiclass Character

- Gain the following traits from the Core Cleric Traits table: Hit Point Die and training with Light and Medium armor and Shields.
- Gain the Cleric's level 1 features, which are listed in the Cleric Features table. See the multiclassing rules in "Character Creation" to determine your available spell slots.

### Cleric Class Features

As a Cleric, you gain the following class features when you reach the specified Cleric levels. These features are listed in the Cleric Features table.
