| 13 | 3 | 9 | 4 | 3 | 2 | — |
| 14 | 3 | 10 | 4 | 3 | 2 | — |
| 15 | 3 | 10 | 4 | 3 | 2 | — |
| 16 | 3 | 11 | 4 | 3 | 3 | — |
| 17 | 3 | 11 | 4 | 3 | 3 | — |
| 18 | 3 | 11 | 4 | 3 | 3 | — |
| 19 | 3 | 12 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 1 |
| 20 | 3 | 13 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 1 |

#### Level 3: War Bond

You can perform a 1-hour ritual, which can be done during a Short Rest, to magically bond yourself to one weapon. Once bonded, you can't be disarmed of that weapon unless you have the Incapacitated condition, and you can summon it into your hand as a Bonus Action if it's on the same plane of existence as you. You can have up to two bonded weapons, but can summon only one at a time; if you bond a third weapon, the bond with the oldest of the previous two ends.

#### Level 7: War Magic

When you take the Attack action on your turn, you can replace one of your attacks with casting a Wizard cantrip that has a casting time of an action.

#### Level 10: Eldritch Strike

When you hit a creature with a weapon attack, that creature has Disadvantage on the next saving throw it makes against a spell you cast before the end of your next turn.

#### Level 15: Arcane Charge

When you use your Action Surge, you can teleport up to 30 feet to an unoccupied space you can see, either before or after the extra action.

#### Level 18: Improved War Magic

When you take the Attack action on your turn, you can replace up to two of your attacks with casting a Wizard cantrip, or a level 1 or 2 Wizard spell, that has a casting time of an action.

### Fighter Subclass: Psi Warrior

_Reinforce Body and Blade with Psionic Force_

A Psi Warrior channels raw psionic power through weapon and armor alike, using telekinetic force to protect allies, strike harder, and move the battlefield itself.

#### Level 3: Psionic Power

You can manifest psionic energy from within yourself, granting yourself these benefits.

_Psionic Energy Dice._ You have a number of Psionic Energy Dice equal to twice your Proficiency Bonus. These dice are d6s, and they turn into d8s at Fighter level 5, d10s at level 11, and d12s at level 17. You regain one expended die when you finish a Short Rest, and you regain all expended dice when you finish a Long Rest.

_Protective Field._ When you or a creature you can see within 30 feet of yourself takes damage, you can take a Reaction and expend one Psionic Energy Die to reduce that damage by the number rolled plus your Intelligence modifier.

_Psionic Strike._ Once per turn, immediately after you hit a creature with a weapon attack, you can expend one Psionic Energy Die to deal extra Force damage to the target equal to the number rolled plus your Intelligence modifier.

_Telekinetic Movement._ As a Magic action, you can move a willing creature or an object weighing up to 300 pounds, up to 30 feet to an unoccupied space you can see. You can use this benefit once without cost, and you regain the free use of it when you finish a Short or Long Rest; you can also spend a Psionic Energy Die to use this benefit again.

#### Level 7: Telekinetic Adept

You gain the following benefits.

_Psi-Powered Leap._ As a Bonus Action, you gain a Fly Speed equal to twice your Speed until the end of the current turn. You can use this benefit once without cost, and you regain the free use of it when you finish a Short or Long Rest; you can also spend a Psionic Energy Die to use this benefit again.

_Telekinetic Thrust._ Immediately after your Psionic Strike hits a creature, you can force that creature to make a Strength saving throw. On a failed save, you knock the target Prone or push it up to 10 feet in a direction of your choice.

#### Level 10: Guarded Mind

You have Resistance to Psychic damage. If you start your turn with the Charmed or Frightened condition, you can spend a Psionic Energy Die (no action required) to end that condition on yourself.

#### Level 15: Bulwark of Force

As a Bonus Action, you grant yourself and any number of creatures you choose within 30 feet of yourself, up to a number of creatures equal to your Intelligence modifier (minimum of one), Half Cover against attacks and other effects for 1 minute. You can use this benefit once without cost, and you regain the free use of it when you finish a Long Rest; you can also spend a Psionic Energy Die to use this benefit again.

#### Level 18: Telekinetic Master

You always have the [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Telekinesis|Telekinesis]] spell prepared. You can cast it without expending a spell slot, and Intelligence is your spellcasting ability for it. While you're concentrating on [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Telekinesis|Telekinesis]] cast in this way, you can make weapon attacks as a Bonus Action.

### Fighter Subclass: Arcane Archer

_Expansion subclass — Xanathar's Guide to Everything (2017). Not one of the four core 2024 PHB Fighter subclasses (Battle Master/Champion/Eldritch Knight/Psi Warrior)—kept here as bonus content._

_Weave Arcane Magic into Every Shot_

Arcane Archers learn a magical tradition that lets them infuse their arrows with a variety of powerful effects, drawn from an ancient elven martial tradition.

#### Level 3: Arcane Archer Lore

You gain proficiency in either the Arcana or Nature skill, and you learn one Wizard cantrip of your choice. Intelligence is your spellcasting ability for it.

#### Level 3: Arcane Shot

You learn two Arcane Shot options of your choice (see the list below). Once per turn when you fire an arrow from a Shortbow or a Longbow as part of the Attack action, you can apply one of your Arcane Shot options to it. You have two uses of this feature, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Short or Long Rest. You learn one additional Arcane Shot option of your choice at Fighter levels 7, 10, 15, and 18; you can also replace a known option with a different one whenever you gain a Fighter level. If an option requires a saving throw, the DC equals 8 plus your Proficiency Bonus and your Intelligence modifier.

**Banishing Arrow.** The target must succeed on a Charisma saving throw or be banished: its Speed becomes 0, it has the Incapacitated condition, and it can't be targeted by any attack or other effect until the end of your next turn, at which point it returns to the space it left or the nearest unoccupied space. At level 18, the target also takes an extra 2d6 Force damage from the shot.

**Beguiling Arrow.** The arrow deals an extra 2d6 Psychic damage (4d6 at level 18). The target must succeed on a Wisdom saving throw or have the Charmed condition, imposed by a creature of your choice within 30 feet of it, until the end of your next turn; the charmer has Advantage on attack rolls against the target for the duration.

**Bursting Arrow.** The arrow deals an extra 2d6 Force damage (4d6 at level 18) to the target, and the same damage to each creature within 10 feet of it.

**Enfeebling Arrow.** The arrow deals an extra 2d6 Necrotic damage (4d6 at level 18). The target must succeed on a Constitution saving throw or have Disadvantage on Strength checks and Strength saving throws, and it deals only half damage with weapon attacks that use Strength, until the start of your next turn.

**Grasping Arrow.** The arrow deals an extra 2d6 Poison damage (4d6 at level 18), and grasping, poisonous vines erupt from it. The target's Speed is reduced by 10 feet until the vines are removed (an action and a successful Strength (Athletics) check against your Arcane Shot save DC), and it takes 2d6 Slashing damage (4d6 at level 18) whenever it moves at least 5 feet on its turn, until the vines are removed. The vines wither away after 1 minute.

**Piercing Arrow.** No attack roll is necessary. The arrow flies in a straight line up to 30 feet through any creatures in its path, ignoring Cover, in a line 1 foot wide. Each creature in the line must make a Dexterity saving throw, taking the arrow's normal damage plus an extra 1d6 Piercing damage (2d6 at level 18) on a failed save, or half as much damage on a successful one.

**Seeking Arrow.** No attack roll is necessary. Choose a creature you've seen before within 300 feet of yourself, even without a line of sight to it, as long as it's on the same plane of existence as you. The arrow flies to the target, ignoring Cover, unless the target is behind Total Cover or out of range, in which case the arrow disappears. The target makes a Dexterity saving throw, taking the arrow's normal damage plus an extra 1d6 Force damage (2d6 at level 18) on a failed save, or half as much damage on a successful one. Whether or not the attack hits, you learn the target's current location if it fails the save.

**Shadow Arrow.** The arrow deals an extra 2d6 Psychic damage (4d6 at level 18) and wreathes the target's head in an illusion of shadow. The target must succeed on a Wisdom saving throw or have Vulnerability to all damage and be unable to see anything more than 5 feet away from it, until the end of your next turn.

#### Level 7: Magic Arrow

Your arrows fired from a Shortbow or Longbow count as magical for the purpose of overcoming Resistance and Immunity to nonmagical attacks and damage.

#### Level 7: Curving Shot

When you make an attack roll with a magic arrow and miss, you can use a Bonus Action to reroll the attack against a different target within 60 feet of the original target and within your weapon's range.

#### Level 15: Ever-Ready Shot

If you roll Initiative and have no uses of Arcane Shot remaining, you regain one use of it.

### Fighter Subclass: Cavalier

_Expansion subclass — Xanathar's Guide to Everything (2017). Not one of the four core 2024 PHB Fighter subclasses—kept here as bonus content._

_Guard Allies as a Mounted Champion_

Cavaliers specialize in mounted combat and in defending their allies, using their reputation and their reach to draw danger onto themselves.

#### Level 3: Bonus Proficiency

You gain proficiency in one of the following skills of your choice: Animal Handling, History, Insight, Performance, or Persuasion. Alternatively, you learn one language of your choice.

#### Level 3: Born to the Saddle

You have Advantage on saving throws made to avoid falling off your mount. If you fall off your mount and descend no more than 10 feet, you can land on your feet if you aren't Incapacitated. Mounting or dismounting a creature costs you only 5 feet of movement, rather than half your Speed.

#### Level 3: Unwavering Mark

As a Bonus Action, or as part of the same attack, you can mark one creature you can see within 5 feet of yourself when you hit it with a Melee weapon attack. The mark lasts until the end of your next turn, but ends early if you have the Incapacitated condition, the target drops to 0 Hit Points, or the target moves more than 5 feet away from you. While within 5 feet of you, a creature marked by you has Disadvantage on any attack roll that doesn't target you. In addition, if a creature marked by you deals damage to anyone other than you, you can take a Reaction to make one Melee weapon attack against the marked creature, with Advantage on the attack roll. You can mark a number of creatures equal to your Strength modifier (minimum of once) before requiring a Long Rest, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

#### Level 7: Warding Maneuver

If you or a creature you can see within 5 feet of yourself is hit by an attack roll, and you're wielding a Melee weapon or a Shield, you can take a Reaction to roll a d8 and add the number rolled to the target's Armor Class against that attack, potentially causing it to miss. If the attack still hits, the target instead has Resistance to the attack's damage. You can use this feature a number of times equal to your Constitution modifier (minimum of once) before requiring a Long Rest, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

#### Level 10: Hold the Line

Creatures provoke an Opportunity Attack from you when they move 5 feet or more while within your reach, even if they take the Disengage action. In addition, when you hit a creature with an Opportunity Attack, that creature's Speed becomes 0 until the end of the current turn.

#### Level 15: Ferocious Charger

If you move at least 10 feet in a straight line immediately before hitting a creature with a Melee weapon attack or an Unarmed Strike as part of the Attack action, you can force that creature to make a Strength saving throw (DC 8 plus your Proficiency Bonus and your Strength modifier). On a failed save, the target has the Prone condition. You can use this benefit only once per turn.

#### Level 18: Vigilant Defender

Whenever a creature other than you that is within 5 feet of yourself and that you can see makes an attack, you can make one Melee weapon attack against that creature as a Reaction, provided you haven't already used your Reaction this round and you don't have the Incapacitated condition. This special Reaction can be triggered on any creature's turn, not just your own, once per turn.

### Fighter Subclass: Rune Knight

_Expansion subclass — Tasha's Cauldron of Everything (2020). Not one of the four core 2024 PHB Fighter subclasses—kept here as bonus content._

_Carve Giant Runes into Weapon and Armor_

Rune Knights study the rune magic of giants, inscribing giant runes onto their gear to grow in size and power during battle.

#### Level 3: Bonus Proficiencies

You gain proficiency with Smith's Tools, and you learn to speak, read, and write Giant.

#### Level 3: Rune Carver

You learn to inscribe magical runes, gaining access to two runes of your choice from the list below. You learn an additional rune of your choice at Fighter levels 7, 10, and 15; you can also replace a rune you know with a different one whenever you gain a Fighter level. If a rune's effect requires a saving throw, the DC equals 8 plus your Proficiency Bonus and your Constitution modifier.

Once per Long Rest per object, you can inscribe one of your runes onto a Weapon, a suit of armor, a Shield, or another wearable object. You can have a number of objects so inscribed at once equal to the number of runes you know, but each rune must be inscribed on a different object; inscribing a rune onto a new object causes the old inscription to fade. You must be within 5 feet of an object to invoke its rune, though the rune's effect can extend beyond that once invoked. If you lose the object, its rune is suppressed until you regain the object or inscribe a new one during a Long Rest.

**Cloud Rune.** While wearing or carrying an object bearing this rune, you have Advantage on Dexterity (Sleight of Hand) checks and Charisma (Deception) checks. In addition, when you or a creature you can see within 30 feet of yourself is hit by an attack roll, you can take a Reaction to invoke the rune and choose a different creature, other than the attacker, within 30 feet of yourself; that creature becomes the target of the attack instead, using the same roll. You can use this benefit a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

**Fire Rune.** While wearing or carrying an object bearing this rune, you have Advantage on Intelligence (Investigation) checks. In addition, when you hit a creature with a weapon attack, you can invoke the rune to summon fiery shackles: the target takes an extra 2d6 Fire damage from the attack and must succeed on a Strength saving throw or have the Restrained condition for 1 minute. While Restrained this way, the target takes 2d6 Fire damage at the start of each of its turns. The target repeats the save at the end of each of its turns, ending the Restrained condition on itself on a success.

**Frost Rune.** While wearing or carrying an object bearing this rune, you have Advantage on Wisdom (Animal Handling) checks and Charisma (Intimidation) checks. In addition, as a Bonus Action, you can invoke the rune to gain a +2 bonus to all ability checks and saving throws that use Strength or Constitution for 10 minutes. You can use this benefit once per Long Rest, or you can expend one use of your Giant's Might feature to invoke it again.

**Hill Rune** (available starting at level 7). While wearing or carrying an object bearing this rune, you have Advantage on saving throws against being Poisoned, and you have Resistance to Poison damage. In addition, as a Bonus Action, you can invoke the rune to gain Resistance to Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing damage for 1 minute. You can use this benefit once per Long Rest, or you can expend one use of your Giant's Might feature to invoke it again.

**Stone Rune.** While wearing or carrying an object bearing this rune, you have Advantage on Intelligence (Insight) checks, and you have Darkvision with a range of 120 feet. In addition, when a creature you can see ends its turn within 30 feet of yourself, you can take a Reaction to invoke the rune, forcing that creature to make a Wisdom saving throw unless it's immune to being Charmed. On a failed save, the creature has the Charmed condition for 1 minute or until it takes damage; while Charmed this way, its Speed becomes 0 and it has the Incapacitated condition, sinking into a dreamy stupor. You can use this benefit once per Long Rest, or you can expend one use of your Giant's Might feature to invoke it again.

**Storm Rune** (available starting at level 7). While wearing or carrying an object bearing this rune, you have Advantage on Charisma (Intimidation) checks, and you can't have the Surprised condition while conscious. In addition, as a Bonus Action, you can invoke the rune for 1 minute; during that time, immediately after any creature within 60 feet of yourself (including yourself) makes an attack roll, an ability check, or a saving throw, you can take a Reaction to grant that creature Advantage or impose Disadvantage on the roll. You can use this benefit once per Long Rest, or you can expend one use of your Giant's Might feature to invoke it again.

#### Level 3: Giant's Might

As a Bonus Action, you magically infuse yourself with giant strength for 1 minute. For the duration, you grow to Large size if there's room for you to do so (your gear grows with you, though any unattuned item that leaves your possession reverts to its normal size), you have Advantage on Strength checks and Strength saving throws, and once per turn when you hit with a weapon or an Unarmed Strike, you can deal an extra 1d6 damage of the weapon's or Unarmed Strike's type. You can use this feature a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

#### Level 7: Runic Shield

When a creature you can see within 60 feet of yourself is about to make an attack roll against a target other than you, you can take a Reaction to invoke a rune, forcing the creature to reroll and use the new roll. You can use this feature a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

#### Level 10: Great Stature

Magic runes have permanently altered your body—you grow 3d4 inches taller, and the extra damage from your Giant's Might feature increases to 1d8.

#### Level 15: Master of Runes

You can invoke each rune you know twice, instead of once, between rests, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Short or Long Rest.

#### Level 18: Runic Juggernaut

The runes on your body glow with power as you grow to unprecedented might. The extra damage from your Giant's Might feature increases to 1d10, and while using that feature, you can grow to Huge size instead of Large (if there's room), and your reach increases by 5 feet while you're Huge in this way.

### Fighter Subclass: Samurai

_Expansion subclass — Xanathar's Guide to Everything (2017). Not one of the four core 2024 PHB Fighter subclasses—kept here as bonus content._

_Fight On through Sheer Force of Will_

Samurai draw on an indomitable fighting spirit that lets them push through wounds and exhaustion that would stop other warriors cold.

#### Level 3: Bonus Proficiency

You gain proficiency in one of the following skills of your choice: History, Insight, Performance, or Persuasion. Alternatively, you learn one language of your choice.

#### Level 3: Fighting Spirit

As a Bonus Action on your turn, you can give yourself Advantage on weapon attack rolls until the end of the current turn. When you do so, you also gain Temporary Hit Points—5 at level 3, 10 at level 10, and 15 at level 15. You can use this feature three times, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

#### Level 7: Elegant Courtier

You gain proficiency in Wisdom saving throws. If you already have this proficiency, you instead gain proficiency in Intelligence or Charisma saving throws (your choice). In addition, when you make a Charisma (Persuasion) check, you can add your Wisdom modifier to the check, in addition to your Charisma modifier.

#### Level 10: Tireless Spirit

If you roll Initiative and have no uses of Fighting Spirit remaining, you regain one use of it.

#### Level 15: Rapid Strike

If you have Advantage on an attack roll against a creature, you can forgo the Advantage for that roll to instead make one additional weapon attack against that creature, as part of the same Attack action, provided you haven't already done so this turn.

#### Level 18: Strength before Death

Your fighting spirit can delay the grip of death. If you're reduced to 0 Hit Points and don't die outright, you can take a Reaction to delay falling Unconscious, and you can immediately take an extra turn, following the normal rules for your turn. If you're still at 0 Hit Points when this extra turn ends, you fall Unconscious as normal, and you must make a Death Saving Throw as normal for that round. Once you use this feature, you can't use it again until you finish a Long Rest.

## Monk

**Core Monk Traits**

| Col 1 | Col 2 |
| --- | --- |
| Primary Ability | Dexterity and Wisdom |
| Hit Point Die | D8 per Monk level |
| Saving Throw Proficiencies | Strength and Dexterity |
| Skill Proficiencies | Choose 2: Acrobatics, Athletics, History, Insight, Religion, or Stealth |
| Weapon Proficiencies | Simple weapons and Martial weapons that have the Light property |
| Tool Proficiencies | Choose one type of Artisan's Tools or Musical Instrument (see Equipment) |
| Armor Training | None |
| Starting Equipment | Choose A or B: (A) Spear, 5 Daggers, Artisan's Tools or Musical Instrument chosen for the tool proficiency above, Explorer's Pack, and 11 GP; or (B) 50 GP |

### Becoming a Monk …

#### As a Level 1 Character

- Gain all the traits in the Core Monk Traits table.
- Gain the Monk's level 1 features, which are listed in the Monk Features table.

#### As a Multiclass Character

- Gain the Hit Point Die trait from the Core Monk Traits table.
- Gain the Monk's level 1 features, which are listed in the Monk Features table.

### Monk Class Features

As a Monk, you gain the following class features when you reach the specified Monk levels. These features are listed in the Monk Features table.

**Monk Features**

| Level | Proficiency Bonus | Class Features | Martial Arts | Focus Points | Unarmored Movement |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | +2 | Martial Arts, Unarmored Defense | 1d6 | — | — |
| 2 | +2 | Monk's Focus, Unarmored Movement, Uncanny Metabolism | 1d6 | 2 | +10 ft. |
| 3 | +2 | Deflect Attacks, Monk Subclass | 1d6 | 3 | +10 ft. |
| 4 | +2 | Ability Score Improvement, Slow Fall | 1d6 | 4 | +10 ft. |
| 5 | +3 | Extra Attack, Stunning Strike | 1d8 | 5 | +10 ft. |
| 6 | +3 | Empowered Strikes, Subclass feature | 1d8 | 6 | +15 ft. |
| 7 | +3 | Evasion | 1d8 | 7 | +15 ft. |
| 8 | +3 | Ability Score Improvement | 1d8 | 8 | +15 ft. |
| 9 | +4 | Acrobatic Movement | 1d8 | 9 | +15 ft. |
| 10 | +4 | Heightened Focus, Self-Restoration | 1d8 | 10 | +20 ft. |
| 11 | +4 | Subclass feature | 1d10 | 11 | +20 ft. |
| 12 | +4 | Ability Score Improvement | 1d10 | 12 | +20 ft. |
| 13 | +5 | Deflect Energy | 1d10 | 13 | +20 ft. |
| 14 | +5 | Disciplined Survivor | 1d10 | 14 | +25 ft. |
| 15 | +5 | Perfect Focus | 1d10 | 15 | +25 ft. |
| 16 | +5 | Ability Score Improvement | 1d10 | 16 | +25 ft. |
| 17 | +6 | Subclass feature | 1d12 | 17 | +25 ft. |
| 18 | +6 | Superior Defense | 1d12 | 18 | +30 ft. |
| 19 | +6 | Epic Boon | 1d12 | 19 | +30 ft. |
| 20 | +6 | Body and Mind | 1d12 | 20 | +30 ft. |

#### Level 1: Martial Arts

Your practice of martial arts gives you mastery of combat styles that use your Unarmed Strike and Monk weapons, which are the following:

- Simple Melee weapons
- Martial Melee weapons that have the Light property

You gain the following benefits while you are unarmed or wielding only Monk weapons and you aren't wearing armor or wielding a Shield.

_Bonus Unarmed Strike._ You can make an Unarmed Strike as a Bonus Action.

_Martial Arts Die._ You can roll 1d6 in place of the normal damage of your Unarmed Strike or Monk weapons. This die changes as you gain Monk levels, as shown in the Martial Arts column of the Monk Features table.

_Dexterous Attacks._ You can use your Dexterity modifier instead of your Strength modifier for the attack and damage rolls of your Unarmed Strikes and Monk weapons. In addition, when you use the Grapple or Shove option of your Unarmed Strike, you can use your Dexterity modifier instead of your Strength modifier to determine the save DC.

#### Level 1: Unarmored Defense

While you aren't wearing armor or wielding a Shield, your base Armor Class equals 10 plus your Dexterity and Wisdom modifiers.

#### Level 2: Monk's Focus

Your focus and martial training allow you to harness a well of extraordinary energy within yourself. This energy is represented by Focus Points. Your Monk level determines the number of points you have, as shown in the Focus Points column of the Monk Features table.

You can expend these points to enhance or fuel certain Monk features. You start knowing three such features: Flurry of Blows, Patient Defense, and Step of the Wind, each of which is detailed below.

When you expend a Focus Point, it is unavailable until you finish a Short or Long Rest, at the end of which you regain all your expended points.

Some features that use Focus Points require your target to make a saving throw. The save DC equals 8 plus your Wisdom modifier and Proficiency Bonus.

**_Flurry of Blows._** You can expend 1 Focus Point to make two Unarmed Strikes as a Bonus Action.

**_Patient Defense._** You can take the Disengage action as a Bonus Action. Alternatively, you can
expend 1 Focus Point to take both the Disengage and the Dodge actions as a Bonus Action.

**_Step of the Wind._** You can take the Dash action as a Bonus Action. Alternatively, you can expend 1 Focus Point to take both the Disengage and Dash actions as a Bonus Action, and your jump distance is doubled for the turn.

#### Level 2: Unarmored Movement

Your speed increases by 10 feet while you aren't wearing armor or wielding a Shield. This bonus increases when you reach certain Monk levels, as shown on the Monk Features table.

#### Level 2: Uncanny Metabolism

When you roll Initiative, you can regain all expended Focus Points. When you do so, roll your Martial Arts die, and regain a number of Hit Points equal to your Monk level plus the number rolled.

Once you use this feature, you can't use it again until you finish a Long Rest.

#### Level 3: Deflect Attacks

When an attack roll hits you and its damage includes Bludgeoning, Piercing, or Slashing damage, you can take a Reaction to reduce the attack's total damage against you. The reduction equals 1d10 plus your Dexterity modifier and Monk level.

If you reduce the damage to 0, you can expend 1 Focus Point to redirect some of the attack's force. If you do so, choose a creature you can see within 5 feet of yourself if the attack was a melee attack or a creature you can see within 60 feet of yourself that isn't behind Total Cover if the attack was a ranged attack. That creature must succeed on a Dexterity saving throw or take damage equal to two rolls of your Martial Arts die plus your Dexterity modifier. The damage is the same type dealt by the attack.

#### Level 3: Monk Subclass

You gain a Monk subclass of your choice. The Warrior of the Open Hand subclass is detailed after this class's description. A subclass is a specialization that grants you features at certain Monk levels. For the rest of your career, you gain each of your subclass's features that are of your Monk level or lower.

#### Level 4: Ability Score Improvement

You gain the Ability Score Improvement feat (see "Feats") or another feat of your choice for which you qualify. You gain this feature again at Monk levels 8, 12, and 16.

#### Level 4: Slow Fall

You can take a Reaction when you fall to reduce any damage you take from the fall by an amount equal to five times your Monk level.

#### Level 5: Extra Attack

You can attack twice instead of once whenever you take the Attack action on your turn.

#### Level 5: Stunning Strike

Once per turn when you hit a creature with a Monk weapon or an Unarmed Strike, you can expend 1 Focus Point to attempt a stunning strike. The target must make a Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, the target has the Stunned condition until the start of your next turn. On a successful save, the target's Speed is halved until the start of your next turn, and the next attack roll made against the target before then has Advantage.

#### Level 6: Empowered Strikes

Whenever you deal damage with your Unarmed Strike, it can deal your choice of Force damage or its normal damage type.

#### Level 7: Evasion

When you're subjected to an effect that allows you to make a Dexterity saving throw to take only half damage, you instead take no damage if you succeed on the saving throw and only half damage if you fail.

You don't benefit from this feature if you have the Incapacitated condition.

#### Level 9: Acrobatic Movement

While you aren't wearing armor or wielding a Shield, you gain the ability to move along vertical surfaces and across liquids on your turn without falling during the movement.

#### Level 10: Heightened Focus

Your Flurry of Blows, Patient Defense, and Step of the Wind gain the following benefits.

**_Flurry of Blows._** You can expend 1 Focus Point to use Flurry of Blows and make three Unarmed Strikes with it instead of two.

**_Patient Defense._** When you expend a Focus Point to use Patient Defense, you gain a number of Temporary Hit Points equal to two rolls of your Martial Arts die.

**_Step of the Wind._** When you expend a Focus Point to use Step of the Wind, you can choose a willing creature within 5 feet of yourself that is Large or smaller. You move the creature with you until the end of your turn. The creature's movement doesn't provoke Opportunity Attacks.

#### Level 10: Self-Restoration

Through sheer force of will, you can remove one of the following conditions from yourself at the end of each of your turns: Charmed, Frightened, or Poisoned.

In addition, forgoing food and drink doesn't give you levels of Exhaustion.

#### Level 13: Deflect Energy

You can now use your Deflect Attacks feature against attacks that deal any damage type, not just Bludgeoning, Piercing, or Slashing.

#### Level 14: Disciplined Survivor

Your physical and mental discipline grant you proficiency in all saving throws.

Additionally, whenever you make a saving throw and fail, you can expend 1 Focus Point to reroll it, and you must use the new roll.

#### Level 15: Perfect Focus

When you roll Initiative and don't use Uncanny Metabolism, you regain expended Focus Points until you have 4 if you have 3 or fewer.

#### Level 18: Superior Defense

At the start of your turn, you can expend 3 Focus Points to bolster yourself against harm for 1 minute or until you have the Incapacitated condition. During that time, you have Resistance to all damage except Force damage.

#### Level 19: Epic Boon

You gain an Epic Boon feat (see "Feats") or another feat of your choice for which you qualify. Boon of Irresistible Offense is recommended.

#### Level 20: Body and Mind

You have developed your body and mind to new heights. Your Dexterity and Wisdom scores increase by 4, to a maximum of 25.

### Monk Subclass: Warrior of Mercy

_Heal or Harm with a Single Touch_

Warriors of Mercy study the body's vital points, mastering the same techniques for both healing and killing. Trained at monasteries devoted to easing suffering, they are equally prepared to end a fight or ease a death.

#### Level 3: Implements of Mercy

You gain proficiency in the Insight and Medicine skills, and you gain proficiency with the Herbalism Kit.

#### Level 3: Hand of Healing

As a Magic action, you can expend 1 Focus Point and touch a creature, restoring Hit Points to it equal to a roll of your Martial Arts die plus your Wisdom modifier.

When you use your Flurry of Blows feature, you can replace one of its Unarmed Strikes with an use of this feature, without spending an additional Focus Point.

#### Level 3: Hand of Harm

Once per turn when you hit a creature with an Unarmed Strike and deal damage, you can expend 1 Focus Point to deal extra Necrotic damage to the target equal to a roll of your Martial Arts die plus your Wisdom modifier.

#### Level 6: Physician's Touch

Your Hand of Harm and Hand of Healing features improve.

_Hand of Harm._ When you use this feature, you can also give the target the Poisoned condition until the end of your next turn.

_Hand of Healing._ When you use this feature, you can also end one of the following conditions on the target: Blinded, Deafened, Paralyzed, Poisoned, or Stunned.

#### Level 11: Flurry of Healing and Harm

When you use your Flurry of Blows feature, you can replace any number of its Unarmed Strikes with uses of your Hand of Healing or Hand of Harm feature without spending Focus Points for them. You can use this benefit a number of times equal to your Wisdom modifier (minimum of once), and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

#### Level 17: Hand of Ultimate Mercy

As a Magic action, you can touch the corpse of a creature that died within the last 24 hours and expend 5 Focus Points to restore it to life with Hit Points equal to 4d10 plus your Wisdom modifier.

Once you use this feature, you can't use it again until you finish a Long Rest.

### Monk Subclass: Warrior of Shadow

_Wield Darkness as Weapon and Refuge_

Warriors of Shadow trained in monasteries touched by the Shadowfell, learning to wield darkness itself—melting into it, striking from within it, and using it to vanish from a fight entirely.

#### Level 3: Shadow Arts

You gain the following benefits.

_Darkvision._ You have Darkvision with a range of 60 feet. If you already have Darkvision, its range increases by 60 feet.

_Minor Illusion._ You learn the [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Minor Illusion|Minor Illusion]] cantrip. Wisdom is your spellcasting ability for it.

_Shadow Magic._ You can expend 1 Focus Point to cast the [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Darkness|Darkness]] spell without needing Material, Somatic, or Verbal components. While you're the one who cast the spell this way, you can see through the Darkness it creates, and you can move the spell's area of magical Darkness up to 60 feet as a Magic action.

#### Level 6: Shadow Step

While you're in Dim Light or Darkness, you can take a Bonus Action to teleport up to 60 feet to an unoccupied space you can see that is also in Dim Light or Darkness. You then have Advantage on the next attack roll you make before the end of the current turn.

#### Level 11: Improved Shadow Step

When you use your Shadow Step feature, you can expend 1 Focus Point to ignore its Dim Light or Darkness requirement, and you can make one Unarmed Strike as part of the same Bonus Action immediately after you teleport.

#### Level 17: Cloak of Shadows

As a Magic action while you're in Dim Light or Darkness, you can expend 3 Focus Points to shroud yourself in shadow for 1 minute or until you end this effect (no action required). While shrouded, you have the following benefits.

_Invisibility._ You have the Invisible condition.

_Shadow Passage._ You can move through the space of a hostile creature, treating that space as Difficult Terrain.

_Free Flurry._ You can use your Flurry of Blows feature without spending a Focus Point.

### Monk Subclass: Warrior of the Elements

_Channel Elemental Force through Unarmed Strikes_

Warriors of the Elements learn to channel acid, cold, fire, lightning, and thunder directly through their bodies, turning every strike into an expression of raw elemental power.

#### Level 3: Elemental Attunement

As a Bonus Action, or at the start of your turn, you can expend 1 Focus Point to attune yourself to the elements for 10 minutes or until you have the Incapacitated condition. While attuned, your Unarmed Strikes have their reach increased by 10 feet, and you can cause them to deal Acid, Cold, Fire, Lightning, or Thunder damage instead of their normal damage type (choose the type each time you attune yourself). When you hit a creature with such a strike, you can force it to make a Strength saving throw; on a failed save, you push the target up to 10 feet in a direction of your choice.

#### Level 3: Manipulate Elements

You learn the [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Elementalism|Elementalism]] cantrip. Wisdom is your spellcasting ability for it.

#### Level 6: Elemental Burst

As a Magic action, you can expend 2 Focus Points to target a point you can see within 120 feet of yourself, creating a 20-foot-radius Sphere of your chosen elemental damage type (from your Elemental Attunement feature) centered on that point. Each creature in the Sphere must make a Dexterity saving throw, taking damage equal to three rolls of your Martial Arts die on a failed save, or half as much damage on a successful save.

#### Level 11: Stride of the Elements

While your Elemental Attunement is active, you have a Fly Speed and a Swim Speed, each equal to your Speed.

#### Level 17: Elemental Epitome

While your Elemental Attunement is active, you gain the following benefits.

_Elemental Resistance._ You have Resistance to your chosen elemental damage type. You can change your choice at the start of each of your turns.

_Elemental Wind._ When you use your Step of the Wind feature to Dash, your movement increases by an extra 20 feet, and creatures within 5 feet of the path you take on that Dash take damage equal to a roll of your Martial Arts die, of your chosen elemental damage type.

_Elemental Strikes._ Once per turn when you hit with an Unarmed Strike, you can add one extra Martial Arts die of your chosen elemental damage type to the damage dealt.

### Monk Subclass: Warrior of the Open Hand

_Master Unarmed Combat Techniques_

Warriors of the Open Hand are masters of unarmed combat. They learn techniques to push and trip their opponents and manipulate their own energy to protect themselves from harm.

#### Level 3: Open Hand Technique

Whenever you hit a creature with an attack granted by your Flurry of Blows, you can impose one of the following effects on that target.

**Addle.** The target can't make Opportunity Attacks until the start of its next turn.

**Push.** The target must succeed on a Strength saving throw or be pushed up to 15 feet away from you.

**Topple.** The target must succeed on a Dexterity saving throw or have the Prone condition.

#### Level 6: Wholeness of Body

You gain the ability to heal yourself. As a Bonus Action, you can roll your Martial Arts die. You regain a number of Hit Points equal to the number rolled plus your Wisdom modifier (minimum of 1 Hit Point regained).

You can use this feature a number of times equal to your Wisdom modifier (minimum of once), and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

#### Level 11: Fleet Step

When you take a Bonus Action other than Step of the Wind, you can also use Step of the Wind immediately after that Bonus Action.

#### Level 17: Quivering Palm

You gain the ability to set up lethal vibrations in someone's body. When you hit a creature with an Unarmed Strike, you can expend 4 Focus Points to start these imperceptible vibrations, which last for a number of days equal to your Monk level. The vibrations are harmless unless you take an action to end them. Alternatively, when you take the Attack action on your turn, you can forgo one of the attacks to end the vibrations. To end them, you and the target must be on the same plane of existence. When you end them, the target must make a Constitution saving throw, taking 10d12 Force damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one.

You can have only one creature under the effect of this feature at a time. You can end the vibrations harmlessly (no action required).

### Monk Subclass: Way of the Ascendant Dragon

_Expansion subclass — Fizban's Treasury of Dragons (2021). Not one of the four core 2024 PHB Monk subclasses (Warrior of Mercy/Shadow/the Elements/the Open Hand)—kept here as bonus content. This subclass and the ones below it use the pre-2024 "ki point" resource; the 2024 PHB renamed this resource "Focus Points" for the core Monk class (see Level 2: Focus, above)—treat "ki point" below as equivalent to a Focus Point._

_Channel the Power of Dragons_

Monks of the Way of the Ascendant Dragon draw on a legacy of draconic martial arts, channeling elemental breath and draconic presence through their unarmed strikes.

#### Level 3: Draconic Disciple

You gain the following benefits.

_Draconic Tongue._ You learn to speak, read, and write Draconic, if you don't already know it.

_Draconic Presence._ Once per Long Rest, you can reroll a failed Charisma check; you must use the new roll.

_Draconic Strikes._ You can change the damage type of your Unarmed Strikes from Bludgeoning to Acid, Cold, Fire, Lightning, or Poison, choosing the type each time you make an Unarmed Strike.

#### Level 3: Breath of the Dragon

As an action, replacing one of your Unarmed Strikes, you can use your Martial Arts die to fuel a draconic breath weapon in a 20-foot Cone or a 30-foot Line that is 5 feet wide (your choice). Each creature in that area must make a Dexterity saving throw against your ki save DC, taking damage equal to two rolls of your Martial Arts die on a failed save, or half as much damage on a successful one. The damage type matches the type you've chosen for your Draconic Strikes feature. You can use this feature a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest; you can also spend 2 ki points to use it again if you've run out. At level 11, the damage increases to three rolls of your Martial Arts die.

#### Level 6: Wings Unfurled

When you use your Step of the Wind feature to Dash or Disengage, you can instead cause spectral wings to unfurl from your back, granting you a Fly Speed equal to your Speed until the end of the current turn. You can use this benefit a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

#### Level 11: Aspect of the Wyrm

As a Bonus Action, you emanate an aura of draconic menace or protection in a 10-foot Emanation for 1 minute or until you have the Incapacitated condition. Each time you use this feature, choose one of the following effects.

_Frightful Presence._ Each hostile creature that starts its turn in the aura must succeed on a Wisdom saving throw against your ki save DC or have the Frightened condition until the aura ends.

_Draconic Resistance._ Choose a damage type: Acid, Cold, Fire, Lightning, or Poison. You and any creatures of your choice in the aura have Resistance to that damage type while the aura lasts.

You can use this feature once, regaining the use when you finish a Long Rest, or you can spend 3 ki points to use it again if you've already used your free use.

#### Level 17: Ascendant Aspect

Your draconic power blazes brighter than ever. You gain the following benefits.

_Augment Breath._ When you use Breath of the Dragon, you can spend 1 additional ki point to increase its area: the Cone becomes 60 feet, or the Line becomes 90 feet long and 5 feet wide.

_Draconic Blindsight._ You have Blindsight with a range of 10 feet.

_Explosive Fury._ When you activate Aspect of the Wyrm, if you choose Frightful Presence, each creature that fails its save also takes damage equal to your Martial Arts die of your chosen damage type. If you choose Draconic Resistance, you and each creature you grant Resistance to also gain Temporary Hit Points equal to your Martial Arts die.

### Monk Subclass: Way of the Astral Self

_Expansion subclass — Tasha's Cauldron of Everything (2020). Not one of the four core 2024 PHB Monk subclasses—kept here as bonus content._

_Manifest a Spectral Astral Form_

Monks of the Way of the Astral Self look inward to a spark of the astral plane within their own soul, learning to manifest spectral arms, a spectral visage, and eventually their whole astral body in battle.

#### Level 3: Arms of the Astral Self

As a Bonus Action, you can spend 1 ki point to summon the arms of your astral self for 10 minutes. When you do so, choose a creature within 10 feet of yourself for each arm; each must succeed on a Dexterity saving throw or take Force damage equal to two rolls of your Martial Arts die plus your Wisdom modifier (a creature can be affected only once, regardless of how many arms you summon at once). While the arms are present, you gain the following benefits.

_Astral Strength._ You use your Wisdom modifier, instead of Strength, for the attack and damage rolls of your Unarmed Strikes.

_Astral Reach._ Your Unarmed Strikes with the arms deal Force damage instead of their normal damage type, and their reach is 5 feet greater than normal.

_Astral Onslaught._ Immediately after you take the Attack action on your turn, you can make one Unarmed Strike with the arms as a Bonus Action.

#### Level 6: Visage of the Astral Self

As a Bonus Action, you can spend 1 ki point to manifest the visage of your astral self for 10 minutes, appearing as a ghostly, otherworldly mask over your face. While present, you gain the following benefits.

_Astral Sight._ You have Darkvision with a range of 120 feet, and you can see in magical Darkness.

_Astral Insight._ You have Advantage on Wisdom (Insight) checks and Charisma (Intimidation) checks.

_Astral Voice._ As a Bonus Action, you can speak with the voice of your astral self, understandable by any creature that knows a language, or amplify your voice to be audible up to 600 feet away for 1 minute.

#### Level 11: Body of the Astral Self

Your ki now fully powers your inner spirit. While your Arms of the Astral Self and Visage of the Astral Self are both present, you gain the following benefits.

_Enveloping Deflection._ Whenever you take Acid, Cold, Fire, Force, Lightning, or Thunder damage, you can take a Reaction to reduce that damage by 1d10 plus your Wisdom modifier.

_Empowered Arms._ Once per turn when you hit a creature with an Unarmed Strike from your astral arms, you can deal extra Force damage to the target equal to one roll of your Martial Arts die.

#### Level 17: Awakened Astral Self

As a Bonus Action, you can spend 5 ki points to summon the arms, visage, and body of your astral self simultaneously, for 10 minutes, without needing to activate each separately. In addition, while your astral arms are present, you gain a +2 bonus to Armor Class, and when you take the Attack action on your turn and attack only with Unarmed Strikes using your astral arms, you can make one additional attack as part of that action.

### Monk Subclass: Way of the Drunken Master

_Expansion subclass — Xanathar's Guide to Everything (2017). Not one of the four core 2024 PHB Monk subclasses—kept here as bonus content._

_Stagger and Weave through Combat_

Monks of the Way of the Drunken Master turn a staggering, off-balance fighting style into a genuine martial art, using unpredictable footwork to slip past an opponent's guard.

#### Level 3: Bonus Proficiencies

You gain proficiency in the Performance skill, if you don't already have it, and proficiency with Brewer's Supplies, if you don't already have it.

#### Level 3: Drunken Technique

Whenever you use Flurry of Blows, you gain the benefit of the Disengage action, and your Speed increases by 10 feet until the end of the current turn.

#### Level 6: Tipsy Sway

You gain the following benefits.

_Leap to Your Feet._ When you have the Prone condition, you can stand up by spending 5 feet of movement, rather than half your Speed.

_Redirect Attack._ When a creature misses you with a melee attack roll, you can spend 1 ki point as a Reaction to cause that attack to hit one creature of your choice, other than yourself and the attacker, that you can see within 5 feet of the attacker.

#### Level 11: Drunkard's Luck

When you make an ability check, an attack roll, or a saving throw and have Disadvantage on the roll, you can spend 2 ki points to cancel the Disadvantage for that roll.

#### Level 17: Intoxicated Frenzy

When you use Flurry of Blows, you can make up to three additional attacks with it, rather than the normal two, for a total of up to five attacks, provided each attack targets a different creature.

### Monk Subclass: Way of the Kensei

_Expansion subclass — Xanathar's Guide to Everything (2017). Not one of the four core 2024 PHB Monk subclasses—kept here as bonus content._

_Master a Chosen Weapon as a Kensei_

Monks of the Way of the Kensei treat a chosen weapon as an extension of their body, achieving the same supernatural mastery over it that other Monks achieve with their bare hands.

#### Level 3: Path of the Kensei

You choose two weapon types to be your kensei weapons: one Melee weapon type and one Ranged weapon type. Neither type can have the Heavy property, and the ranged type must be a Martial Ranged weapon. Weapons of your chosen types are Monk weapons for you, in addition to your Unarmed Strikes and Shortswords, and you gain proficiency with them if you lack it. You gain access to additional kensei weapon types of your choice when you reach certain Monk levels: two additional types at level 6, and one more at both level 11 and level 17.

#### Level 3: Agile Parry

When you use your action to attack with a Melee kensei weapon, or to make an Unarmed Strike, and you're holding a kensei weapon, you can also use it to defend yourself. You gain a +2 bonus to Armor Class until the start of your next turn, but you don't gain this bonus if you're using a weapon that requires two hands.

#### Level 3: Kensei's Shot

You can use a Bonus Action to focus your ki into one Ranged kensei weapon attack, provided you haven't already used a Bonus Action this turn. If you hit a target with a Ranged kensei weapon attack this turn, that target takes an extra 1d4 damage of the weapon's type.

#### Level 3: Way of the Brush

You gain proficiency with Painter's Supplies or Calligrapher's Supplies (your choice).

#### Level 6: One with the Blade

Your kensei weapon attacks now count as magical for the purpose of overcoming Resistance and Immunity to nonmagical attacks and damage. In addition, once per turn when you hit a creature with a kensei weapon, you can spend 1 ki point to deal extra damage to the target equal to your Martial Arts die.

#### Level 11: Sharpen the Blade

As a Bonus Action, you can expend up to 3 ki points to magically sharpen one kensei weapon you're holding. For 1 minute, that weapon gains a bonus to attack and damage rolls equal to the number of ki points you spent, and it's considered magical if it isn't already.

#### Level 17: Unerring Accuracy

If you miss with an attack roll using a Monk weapon on your turn, you can reroll it. You can use this feature only once per turn.

### Monk Subclass: Way of the Sun Soul

_Expansion subclass — Xanathar's Guide to Everything (2017), originally introduced in the Sword Coast Adventurer's Guide (2015). Not one of the four core 2024 PHB Monk subclasses—kept here as bonus content._

_Hurl Bolts of Radiant Light_

Monks of the Way of the Sun Soul learn to project their own inner light outward, hurling bolts of pure radiance and eventually wreathing themselves in a shield of searing light.

#### Level 3: Radiant Sun Bolt

As part of your action, you can make a ranged spell attack, using Dexterity, at one creature you can see within 30 feet of yourself, requiring no ammunition or spell components to do so. On a hit, the target takes Radiant damage equal to your Martial Arts die. You can use your Flurry of Blows feature to make two of these attacks as a Bonus Action, instead of two Unarmed Strikes.

#### Level 6: Searing Arc Strike

Immediately after you take the Attack action on your turn, you can spend 2 ki points to cast [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Burning Hands|Burning Hands]] as a Bonus Action, using your ki save DC as the spell's save DC. You can increase the effective spell slot level of the [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Burning Hands|Burning Hands]] cast this way by spending 1 additional ki point per level increase, up to a maximum spell level equal to half your Monk level (round down).

#### Level 11: Searing Sunburst

As an action, you create an orb of light at a point you choose within 150 feet of yourself. The orb flashes with a burst of radiant light. Each creature in a 20-foot-radius Sphere centered on that point must make a Constitution saving throw against your ki save DC, taking 2d6 Radiant damage on a failed save, or half as much damage on a successful one. You can increase the damage by 2d6, up to three additional times, by spending 1 additional ki point per increase.

#### Level 17: Sun Shield

You are surrounded by a radiant light that harms those who would harm you. You shed Bright Light in a 30-foot radius and Dim Light for an additional 30 feet. You can extinguish or restore this light as a Bonus Action. Whenever a creature within 5 feet of yourself hits you with a melee attack, you can take a Reaction to deal Radiant damage to that creature equal to 5 plus your Wisdom modifier.

## Paladin

**Core Paladin Traits**

| Col 1 | Col 2 |
| --- | --- |
| Primary Ability | Strength and Charisma |
| Hit Point Die | D10 per Paladin level |
| Saving Throw Proficiencies | Wisdom and Charisma |
| Skill Proficiencies | Choose 2: Athletics, Insight, Intimidation, Medicine, Persuasion, or Religion |
| Weapon Proficiencies | Simple and Martial weapons |
| Armor Training | Light, Medium, and Heavy armor and Shields |
| Starting Equipment | Choose A or B: (A) Chain Mail, Shield, Longsword, 6 Javelins, Holy Symbol, Priest's Pack, and 9 GP; or (B) 150 GP |

### Becoming a Paladin …

#### As a Level 1 Character

• Gain all the traits in the Core Paladin Traits table.

• Gain the Paladin's level 1 features, which are listed in the Paladin Features table.

#### As a Multiclass Character

• Gain the following traits from the Core Paladin Traits table: Hit Point Die, proficiency with Martial weapons, and training with Light and Medium armor and Shields.

• Gain the Paladin's level 1 features, which are listed in the Paladin Features table. See the multiclassing rules in "Character Creation" to determine your available spell slots.

### Paladin Class Features

As a Paladin, you gain the following class features when you reach the specified Paladin levels. These features are listed in the Paladin Features table.

**Paladin Features**

| Level | Proficiency Bonus | Class Features | Channel Divinity | Prepared Spells | Lvl 1 Slots | Lvl 2 Slots | Lvl 3 Slots | Lvl 4 Slots | Lvl 5 Slots |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | +2 | Lay On Hands, Spellcasting, Weapon Mastery | — | 2 | 2 | — | — | — | — |
| 2 | +2 | Fighting Style, Paladin's Smite | — | 3 | 2 | — | — | — | — |
| 3 | +2 | Channel Divinity, Paladin Subclass | 2 | 4 | 3 | — | — | — | — |
| 4 | +2 | Ability Score Improvement | 2 | 5 | 3 | — | — | — | — |
| 5 | +3 | Extra Attack, Faithful Steed | 2 | 6 | 4 | 2 | — | — | — |
| 6 | +3 | Aura of Protection | 2 | 6 | 4 | 2 | — | — | — |
| 7 | +3 | Subclass feature | 2 | 7 | 4 | 3 | — | — | — |
| 8 | +3 | Ability Score Improvement | 2 | 7 | 4 | 3 | — | — | — |
| 9 | +4 | Abjure Foes | 2 | 9 | 4 | 3 | 2 | — | — |
| 10 | +4 | Aura of Courage | 2 | 9 | 4 | 3 | 2 | — | — |
| 11 | +4 | Radiant Strikes | 3 | 10 | 4 | 3 | 3 | — | — |
| 12 | +4 | Ability Score Improvement | 3 | 10 | 4 | 3 | 3 | — | — |
| 13 | +5 | — | 3 | 11 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 1 | — |
| 14 | +5 | Restoring Touch | 3 | 11 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 1 | — |
| 15 | +5 | Subclass feature | 3 | 12 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 2 | — |
| 16 | +5 | Ability Score Improvement | 3 | 12 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 2 | — |
| 17 | +6 | — | 3 | 14 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 1 |
| 18 | +6 | Aura Expansion | 3 | 14 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 1 |
| 19 | +6 | Epic Boon | 3 | 15 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 2 |
| 20 | +6 | Subclass feature | 3 | 15 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 2 |

#### Level 1: Lay On Hands

Your blessed touch can heal wounds. You have a pool of healing power that replenishes when you finish a Long Rest. With that pool, you can restore a total number of Hit Points equal to five times your Paladin level.

As a Bonus Action, you can touch a creature (which could be yourself) and draw power from the pool of healing to restore a number of Hit Points to that creature, up to the maximum amount remaining in the pool.

You can also expend 5 Hit Points from the pool of healing power to remove the Poisoned condition from the creature; those points don't also restore Hit Points to the creature.

#### Level 1: Spellcasting

You have learned to cast spells through prayer and meditation. See "Spells" for the rules on spellcasting. The information below details how you use those rules with Paladin spells, which appear in the Paladin spell list later in the class's description.

**Spell Slots.** The Paladin Features table shows how many spell slots you have to cast your level 1+ spells. You regain all expended slots when you finish a Long Rest.

**Prepared Spells of Level 1+.** You prepare the list of level 1+ spells that are available for you to cast with this feature. To start, choose two level 1 Paladin spells. [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Heroism|Heroism]] and [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Searing Smite|Searing Smite]] are recommended.

The number of spells on your list increases as you gain Paladin levels, as shown in the Prepared Spells column of the Paladin Features table. Whenever that number increases, choose additional Paladin spells until the number of spells on your list matches the number in the Paladin Features table. The chosen spells must be of a level for which you have spell slots. For example, if you're a level 5 Paladin, your list of prepared spells can include six Paladin spells of level 1 or 2 in any combination.

If another Paladin feature gives you spells that you always have prepared, those spells don't count against the number of spells you can prepare with this feature, but those spells otherwise count as Paladin spells for you.

**Changing Your Prepared Spells.** Whenever you finish a Long Rest, you can replace one spell on your list with another Paladin spell for which you have spell slots.

**Spellcasting Ability.** Charisma is your spellcasting ability for your Paladin spells.

**Spellcasting Focus.** You can use a Holy Symbol as a Spellcasting Focus for your Paladin spells.

#### Level 1: Weapon Mastery

Your training with weapons allows you to use the mastery properties of two kinds of weapons of your choice with which you have proficiency, such as Longswords and Javelins.

Whenever you finish a Long Rest, you can change the kinds of weapons you chose. For example, you could switch to using the mastery properties of Halberds and Flails.

#### Level 2: Fighting Style

You gain a Fighting Style feat of your choice (see "Feats" for feats). Instead of choosing one of those feats, you can choose the option below.

**Blessed Warrior.** You learn two Cleric cantrips of your choice (see the Cleric class's section for a list of Cleric spells). [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Guidance|Guidance]] and [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Sacred Flame|Sacred Flame]] are recommended. The chosen cantrips count as Paladin spells for you, and Charisma is your spellcasting ability for them. Whenever you gain a Paladin level, you can replace one of these cantrips with another Cleric cantrip.

#### Level 2: Paladin's Smite

You always have the [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Divine Smite|Divine Smite]] spell prepared. In addition, you can cast it without expending a spell slot, but you must finish a Long Rest before you can cast it in this way again.

#### Level 3: Channel Divinity

You can channel divine energy directly from the Outer Planes, using it to fuel magical effects. You start with one such effect: Divine Sense, which is described below. Other Paladin features give additional Channel Divinity effect options. Each time you use this class's Channel Divinity, you choose which effect from this class to create.

You can use this class's Channel Divinity twice. You regain one of its expended uses when you finish a Short Rest, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest. You gain an additional use when you reach Paladin level 11.

> ### Breaking Your Oath
>
> A Paladin tries to hold to the highest standards of conduct, but even the most dedicated are fallible. Sometimes a Paladin transgresses their oath.
>
> A Paladin who has broken a vow typically seeks absolution, spending an all-night vigil as a sign of penitence or undertaking a fast. After a rite of forgiveness, the Paladin starts fresh.
>
> If your Paladin unrepentantly violates their oath, talk to your GM. Your Paladin should probably take a more appropriate subclass or even abandon the class and adopt another one.
> If a Channel Divinity effect requires a saving throw, the DC equals the spell save DC from this class's Spellcasting feature.

_Divine Sense._ As a Bonus Action, you can open your awareness to detect Celestials, Fiends, and Undead. For the next 10 minutes or until you have the Incapacitated condition, you know the location of any creature of those types within 60 feet of yourself, and you know its creature type. Within the same radius, you also detect the presence of any place or object that has been consecrated or desecrated, as with the [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Hallow|Hallow]] spell.

#### Level 3: Paladin Subclass

You gain a Paladin subclass of your choice. The Oath of Devotion subclass is detailed after this class's description. A subclass is a specialization that grants you features at certain Paladin levels. For the rest of your career, you gain each of your subclass's features that are of your Paladin level or lower.

#### Level 4: Ability Score Improvement

You gain the Ability Score Improvement feat (see "Feats") or another feat of your choice for which you qualify. You gain this feature again at Paladin levels 8, 12, and 16.

#### Level 5: Extra Attack

You can attack twice instead of once whenever you take the Attack action on your turn.

#### Level 5: Faithful Steed

You can call on the aid of an otherworldly steed. You always have the [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Find Steed|Find Steed]] spell prepared.

You can also cast the spell once without expending a spell slot, and you regain the ability to do so when you finish a Long Rest.

#### Level 6: Aura of Protection

You radiate a protective, unseeable aura in a 10-foot Emanation that originates from you. The aura is inactive while you have the Incapacitated condition.

You and your allies in the aura gain a bonus to saving throws equal to your Charisma modifier (minimum bonus of +1).

If another Paladin is present, a creature can benefit from only one Aura of Protection at a time; the creature chooses which aura while in them.

#### Level 9: Abjure Foes

As a Magic action, you can expend one use of this class's Channel Divinity to overwhelm foes with awe. As you present your Holy Symbol or weapon, you can target a number of creatures equal to your Charisma modifier (minimum of one creature) that you can see within 60 feet of yourself. Each target must succeed on a Wisdom saving throw or have the Frightened condition for 1 minute or until it takes any damage. While Frightened in this way, a target can do only one of the following on its turns: move, take an action, or take a Bonus Action.

#### Level 10: Aura of Courage

You and your allies have Immunity to the Frightened condition while in your Aura of Protection. If a Frightened ally enters the aura, that condition has no effect on that ally while there.

#### Level 11: Radiant Strikes

Your strikes now carry supernatural power. When you hit a target with an attack roll using a Melee weapon or an Unarmed Strike, the target takes an extra 1d8 Radiant damage.

#### Level 14: Restoring Touch

When you use Lay On Hands on a creature, you can also remove one or more of the following conditions from the creature: Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Frightened, Paralyzed, or Stunned. You must expend 5 Hit Points from the healing pool of Lay On Hands for each of these conditions you remove; those points don't also restore Hit Points to the creature.

#### Level 18: Aura Expansion

Your Aura of Protection is now a 30-foot Emanation.

#### Level 19: Epic Boon

You gain an Epic Boon feat (see "Feats") or another feat of your choice for which you qualify. Boon of Truesight is recommended.
