### Wizard Subclass: Abjurer

_Block, Banish, and Protect with Ordered Magic_

Your studies focus on magic that blocks, banishes, and protects—closing portals, exorcising spirits, and shielding yourself and your allies from harm. You are the party's answer to hostile magic.

#### Level 3: Abjuration Savant

Choose two Wizard spells from the Abjuration school, each of which must be no higher than level 2, and add them to your spellbook for free.

In addition, whenever you gain access to a new level of spell slots in this class, you can add one Wizard spell from the Abjuration school to your spellbook for free. The chosen spell must be of a level for which you have spell slots.

#### Level 3: Arcane Ward

When you cast an Abjuration spell using a spell slot, you can weave a strand of its magic into a ward that protects you. The ward lasts until you finish a Long Rest, has a Hit Point maximum equal to twice your Wizard level plus your Intelligence modifier, and you can create only one such ward at a time.

Whenever you take damage while the ward is active, it absorbs the damage instead of you, applying any Resistance or Vulnerability you have before reducing the ward's Hit Points. If damage reduces the ward to 0 Hit Points, you take any remaining damage.

Whenever you cast an Abjuration spell using a spell slot, the ward regains Hit Points equal to twice the level of the spell slot expended. Alternatively, as a Bonus Action, you can expend a spell slot to have the ward regain Hit Points equal to twice the level of the slot expended, without casting a spell.

#### Level 6: Projected Ward

When a creature you can see within 30 feet of yourself takes damage, you can take a Reaction to cause your Arcane Ward to absorb that damage instead, applying the protected creature's own Resistance or Vulnerability before reducing the ward's Hit Points.

#### Level 10: Spell Breaker

You always have [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Counterspell|Counterspell]] and [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Dispel Magic|Dispel Magic]] prepared, and they don't count against the number of spells you can have prepared. You can cast [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Dispel Magic|Dispel Magic]] as a Bonus Action, and when you do, you can add your Proficiency Bonus to the ability check made to determine whether it successfully counters a spell of level 6 or higher.

If your casting of [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Counterspell|Counterspell]] or [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Dispel Magic|Dispel Magic]] fails to counter or dispel a spell, the spell slot you expended isn't spent.

#### Level 14: Spell Resistance

You have Advantage on saving throws against spells, and you have Resistance to damage from spells.

### Wizard Subclass: Diviner

_Pierce the Veils of Time, Distance, and Thought_

Your studies are directed at piercing the veils of time, distance, and thought. Foresight and remote knowledge are your specialty, and your magic can even rewrite the outcome of a die roll.

#### Level 3: Divination Savant

Choose two Wizard spells from the Divination school, each of which must be no higher than level 2, and add them to your spellbook for free.

In addition, whenever you gain access to a new level of spell slots in this class, you can add one Wizard spell from the Divination school to your spellbook for free. The chosen spell must be of a level for which you have spell slots.

#### Level 3: Portent

Whenever you finish a Long Rest, roll two d20s and record the numbers rolled. You can replace any D20 Test made by you or a creature you can see with one of these foretelling rolls; you must choose to do so before the roll is made (or before its outcome is known), and you can replace only one roll in this way on each of your turns. Each foretelling roll can be used only once. When you finish a Long Rest, you lose any unused foretelling rolls.

#### Level 6: Expert Divination

When you cast a Divination spell using a spell slot of level 2 or higher, you regain one expended spell slot. The regained slot must be of a level lower than the slot you just expended, and it can't be higher than level 5.

#### Level 10: The Third Eye

As a Bonus Action, you can heighten your senses with one of the following benefits, which lasts until you start a Short or Long Rest. Once you use this feature, you can't use it again until you finish a Short or Long Rest.

**Darkvision.** You gain Darkvision with a range of 120 feet.

**Greater Comprehension.** You can read any language.

**See Invisibility.** You can see invisible creatures and objects as though they weren't invisible.

#### Level 14: Greater Portent

You roll three d20s for your Portent feature, rather than two.

### Wizard Subclass: Evoker

_Create Explosive Elemental Effects_

Your studies focus on magic that creates powerful elemental effects such as bitter cold, searing flame, rolling thunder, crackling lightning, and burning acid. Some Evokers find employment in military forces, serving as artillery to blast armies from afar. Others use their power to protect others, while some seek their own gain.

#### Level 3: Evocation Savant

Choose two Wizard spells from the Evocation school, each of which must be no higher than level 2, and add them to your spellbook for free.

In addition, whenever you gain access to a new level of spell slots in this class, you can add one Wizard spell from the Evocation school to your spellbook for free. The chosen spell must be of a level for which you have spell slots.

#### Level 3: Potent Cantrip

Your damaging cantrips affect even creatures that avoid the brunt of the effect. When you cast a cantrip at a creature and you miss with the attack roll or the target succeeds on a saving throw against the cantrip, the target takes half the cantrip's damage (if any) but suffers no additional effect from the cantrip.

#### Level 6: Sculpt Spells

You can create pockets of relative safety within the effects of your evocations. When you cast an Evocation spell that affects other creatures that you can see, you can choose a number of them equal to 1 plus the spell's level. The chosen creatures automatically succeed on their saving throws against the spell, and they take no damage if they would normally take half damage on a successful save.

#### Level 10: Empowered Evocation

Whenever you cast a Wizard spell from the Evocation school, you can add your Intelligence modifier to one damage roll of that spell.

#### Level 14: Overchannel

You can increase the power of your spells. When you cast a Wizard spell with a spell slot of levels 1–5 that deals damage, you can deal maximum damage with that spell on the turn you cast it.

The first time you do so, you suffer no adverse effect. If you use this feature again before you finish a Long Rest, you take 2d12 Necrotic damage for each level of the spell slot immediately after you cast it. This damage ignores Resistance and Immunity.

Each time you use this feature again before finishing a Long Rest, the Necrotic damage per spell level increases by 1d12.

### Wizard Subclass: Illusionist

_Bend Perception into Something Real_

Your studies bend perception itself—sound, light, and appearance—and your training eventually lets your illusions blur into something that can briefly touch reality.

#### Level 3: Illusion Savant

Choose two Wizard spells from the Illusion school, each of which must be no higher than level 2, and add them to your spellbook for free.

In addition, whenever you gain access to a new level of spell slots in this class, you can add one Wizard spell from the Illusion school to your spellbook for free. The chosen spell must be of a level for which you have spell slots.

#### Level 3: Improved Illusions

You can cast spells from the Illusion school without providing their Verbal components, and if an Illusion spell you cast normally has a range of 10 feet or greater, its range increases by 60 feet.

You also know the [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Minor Illusion|Minor Illusion]] cantrip; if you already know it, you instead learn a different Wizard cantrip of your choice. This cantrip doesn't count against the number of cantrips you know. In addition, when you cast [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Minor Illusion|Minor Illusion]], you can create both a sound and an image with a single casting of the spell, and you can cast it as a Bonus Action.

#### Level 6: Phantasmal Creatures

You always have the [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Summon Beast|Summon Beast]] and [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Summon Fey|Summon Fey]] spells prepared, and they don't count against the number of spells you can have prepared. When you cast either spell, you can change its school from Conjuration to Illusion, in which case the summoned creature appears spectral.

You can also cast either spell, in its Illusion form, without expending a spell slot, but doing so halves the summoned creature's Hit Point maximum. Once you cast a spell in this way, you can't do so again until you finish a Long Rest.

#### Level 10: Illusory Self

When a creature hits you with an attack roll, you can take a Reaction to interpose an illusory duplicate of yourself between the attacker and yourself. The triggering attack automatically misses you, and the illusion then dissipates.

Once you use this feature, you can't use it again until you finish a Short or Long Rest, unless you expend a spell slot of level 2 or higher (no action required) to use it again.

#### Level 14: Illusory Reality

When you cast an Illusion spell using a spell slot, you can use a Bonus Action to make one inanimate, nonmagical object that is part of the illusion real. The object remains real for 1 minute or until the spell ends. While real, the object can't deal damage or otherwise directly harm anyone.

### Wizard Subclass: Bladesinging

_Expansion subclass — Tasha's Cauldron of Everything (2020). Not one of the four core 2024 PHB Wizard subclasses (Abjurer/Diviner/Evoker/Illusionist)—kept here as bonus content._

_Blend Swordplay with Wizardry_

Bladesingers practice an elven martial art that weaves spellcasting and swordplay into a single graceful discipline, becoming just as dangerous in melee as at range.

#### Level 3: Training in War and Song

You gain proficiency with Light armor, with one type of one-handed Melee weapon of your choice, and with the Performance skill, if you don't already have it.

#### Level 3: Bladesong

As a Bonus Action, provided you aren't wearing Medium or Heavy armor or using a Shield, you can invoke a Bladesong that lasts for 1 minute; it ends early if you don a Shield or Medium or Heavy armor, if you have the Incapacitated condition, or if you use two hands to make an attack with a weapon. While your Bladesong is active, you gain the following benefits: a bonus to Armor Class equal to your Intelligence modifier (minimum of +1); your Speed increases by 10 feet; you have Advantage on Dexterity (Acrobatics) checks; and you gain a bonus to Constitution saving throws you make to maintain Concentration equal to your Intelligence modifier (minimum of +1). You can use this feature a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

#### Level 6: Extra Attack

You can attack twice, instead of once, whenever you take the Attack action on your turn. You can replace one of these attacks with a cantrip you know.

#### Level 10: Song of Defense

While your Bladesong is active, you can take a Reaction when you take damage to expend a spell slot and reduce that damage to yourself by an amount equal to five times the slot's level.

#### Level 14: Song of Victory

While your Bladesong is active, you add your Intelligence modifier (minimum of +1) to the damage of your Melee weapon attacks.

### Wizard Subclass: Order of Scribes

_Expansion subclass — Tasha's Cauldron of Everything (2020). Not one of the four core 2024 PHB Wizard subclasses—kept here as bonus content._

_Bind a Sentient Spellbook as Your Companion_

Wizards of the Order of Scribes devote themselves to the perfection of the written word, awakening their own spellbook into a sentient companion.

#### Level 3: Wizardly Quill

You gain a magic quill, which you can use as a Spellcasting Focus for your Wizard spells. It never needs ink, producing its own in a color of your choice as you write, and using it to copy a spell into your spellbook takes only 2 minutes per level of the spell, instead of the normal 2 hours per level. As a Bonus Action, you can erase text written by the quill within 5 feet of yourself.

#### Level 3: Awakened Spellbook

Your spellbook becomes a sentient magical companion and serves as your Spellcasting Focus. While holding or carrying it, you gain the following benefits.

_Wisdom of the Scribes._ When you cast a ritual spell from your spellbook, you don't add the normal 10 minutes to its casting time.

_Manifold Damage._ When you cast a Wizard spell using a spell slot, you can change that spell's damage type to a damage type used by a different spell in your spellbook, provided that other spell is of the same level as the slot you expended.

#### Level 6: Manifest Mind

As a Bonus Action, you conjure a spectral, sentient copy of your spellbook's mind, called a Manifest Mind, in an unoccupied space you can see within 60 feet of yourself. It has Darkvision with a range of 60 feet, can see and hear normally, and you can cast spells as though you were in its space, using your own senses. It can move up to 30 feet on your turn at your mental command, and it disappears if it moves more than 300 feet from you or if you dismiss it (a Bonus Action). You can use this feature a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

#### Level 10: Master Scrivener

During a Long Rest, you can use your Wizardly Quill to create one Spell Scroll, at no material cost, copying a level 1 or 2 spell from your spellbook that has a casting time of an action. The scroll functions as though the spell were cast using a spell slot one level higher than normal for that scroll. You can have only one such scroll at a time.

#### Level 14: One with the Word

While holding or carrying your spellbook, you have Advantage on Intelligence (Arcana) checks. In addition, when you would take damage, you can take a Reaction to have your Manifest Mind, if it exists within range, sacrifice itself to negate that damage entirely. If you do, you must give up the ability to cast spells with a combined level equal to or greater than a roll of 3d6, chosen from among the spells you know or have prepared, until you finish 1d6 Long Rests.

### Wizard Subclass: War Magic

_Expansion subclass — Xanathar's Guide to Everything (2017). Not one of the four core 2024 PHB Wizard subclasses—kept here as bonus content._

_Weave Battlefield Tactics into Spellcraft_

Wizards of War Magic train at academies that treat magic as a weapon of war, learning to protect themselves and their allies in the chaos of battle.

#### Level 3: Arcane Deflection

As a Reaction when you're hit by an attack or when you fail a saving throw, you gain a +2 bonus to Armor Class against that attack, potentially causing it to miss, or a +4 bonus to that saving throw. If you use this feature, you can't cast a spell, other than a cantrip, until the end of your next turn.

#### Level 3: Tactical Wit

You add your Intelligence modifier to your Initiative rolls.

#### Level 6: Power Surge

You gain a pool of power surges, with a maximum number equal to your Intelligence modifier (minimum of 1). You regain 1 surge whenever you successfully cast [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Dispel Magic|Dispel Magic]] or [[z_5.5e Compendium/Spells#Counterspell|Counterspell]], and you regain all expended surges when you finish a Long Rest. Once per turn when you deal damage to a creature or an object with a Wizard spell, you can spend 1 surge to deal extra Force damage to it equal to half your Wizard level.

#### Level 10: Durable Magic

While you're concentrating on a spell, you gain a +2 bonus to Armor Class and to all saving throws.

#### Level 14: Deflecting Shroud

Whenever you use your Arcane Deflection feature, you can have magical energy arc outward: up to three creatures of your choice within 60 feet of yourself each take Force damage equal to half your Wizard level.
